## Weights of evidence

**R06. The model's step takes the first verdict of each item, while help — a rung of the ladder before it, a technique on request, a solution already seen — only keeps "mastered" from being set. Each of the N answers of one attempt weighs 1/(1 + (N − 1)/3), one piece of evidence gives one step, and "mastered" is decided by pieces of evidence. The item's primary skills get 1/J of the step each, the supporting ones nothing. A key from the author, from a solver or from an adult weighs 1, a key or skills from AI — 0.** The author's answers of 2026-11-02 in planning T03 (RUN.md): the first is about the order with the move to the tag, and it is R05, and the other eleven follow the plan's recommendations. The model is [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.3; the sources are section 4 there.
- **What is decided:**
  - an item's answer is its first verdict in the attempt, "correct" or "not yet". Later answers of the same item do not go into the step, and the number of the hint k does not change the step. An item first answered after a rung goes with help;
  - help is a rung of the ladder before the item's first verdict, a technique on request anywhere in the attempt, a task's solution that has already gone to the device — that is, almost any repeat of a solved task, not of a template, — and a sketch, when it appears. An answer with help goes into the step, into the counters and into the stability as it is, but does not set "mastered". A technique at a step is not help;
  - a series is all the items of one attempt: each of the N answers weighs u = 1/(1 + (N − 1) · ρ), ρ = 1/3. For θ and n, N is the evidence's items with an answer; for a skill's δ and m, the items with an answer where it is primary. The format needs no mark of items being of one type, and a task of one item weighs 1;
  - one piece of evidence is one step: every P comes from the state before it, and the shifts add up. n, m, the five answers of the trial series and the five answers before "mastered" are sums of the weights u. The trial series is whole pieces of evidence of the module while n < 5;
  - "mastered" goes by pieces of evidence. A piece of evidence fails a skill if the skill's correct answers in it are fewer than half; two failures in a row remove "mastered", and a piece of evidence without a failure sets it if it has a correct answer without help and m ≥ 5;
  - each of the item's J primary skills gets 1/J of the step of δ, and m grows by the whole u. Supporting skills get nothing — no step, no counters, no stability;
  - outcomes go by items: at "solution shown", empty and unsubmitted items are "not solved", S = 0, and an item that before the showing had only the verdict "unreadable" gives nothing: it is not an error (R04 spec); at "left unfinished" only the items with a verdict go; "unchecked" weighs nothing by itself, and the verdicts of the items before it go. Practice belongs to the skills that have an answer in the piece of evidence;
  - the stability of a skill whose answers are not all correct changes by the share of correct ones S̄: τ := max(τ₀; τ · (1 + g · (1 − R))^S̄ · (1/2)^(1 − S̄));
  - provenance: a key from the author, from a solver or entered by an adult weighs 1, and a key or skill tags derived by AI weigh 0. An adult's key also needs a band, a difficulty and skills not from AI. A trap that the program found weighs 1 in the count of traps and goes to the skills of its `skills` in the catalogue, and without `skills`, to the module; a trap that AI supposed weighs 0: the piece of evidence does not carry it;
  - a probe counts as any task, without a lift;
  - "explain differently" and a task being a segment of a lesson do not change the weights, and there is no place for the weight of feedback by rubric.
- **A proposal of the plan and of the execution, not the author's answer:**
  - K_θ and K_δ go by n and m before the piece of evidence, and v after it; the likelihood of the trial series is weighted, and the module's state stores u for each of its answers;
  - removing "mastered" is checked before setting it, and one piece of evidence does not do both;
  - frequent traps: for each trap of a skill, the number of pieces of evidence where the program found it, once per piece of evidence, and the time of the last one; a trap with skills without a state is counted at the module, as one without `skills` is, and which traps are frequent is decided by the readers;
  - the model counts u, J and the series by the skill IDs recorded in the piece of evidence, before the transfer by `retired_skills`, and the sums in a canonical order: items by place, skills by ID;
  - 1/J is the derivative of the log-likelihood by the skill's δ, not a quote: Pelánek 2016 in the multidimensional extension of Elo keeps each task in one knowledge component.
- **What R06 does not decide:**
  - how a piece of evidence carries the first verdicts, help, provenance and traps, whether it carries facts without weight for future versions, and with what weight the transfer by `retired_skills` goes — T04;
  - the types — T05;
  - knowing an anchor skill — T09;
  - which traps are frequent, when to take a skill off pause and after how long to give a task again — `engine`.

  What R06 hands them is [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 2.4–2.6.

— *Why:*
- **The first verdict.** The guessing floor c and P describe the first try: after a wrong answer and a hint the probability is different, and there are no data on what it is. So it is in standalone too, "The answer is recorded once" (R101 standalone), and KDD Cup 2010 measured models by "Correct First Attempt" — "the tutor's evaluation of the student's first attempt on the step—1 if correct, 0 if an error" ([Data Format](https://pslcdatashop.web.cmu.edu/KDDCup/rules_data_format.jsp)). The log carries how many wrong answers there were before the first verdict, and a new version can read partial credit by recomputation.
- **Help only in "mastered".** In standalone "The hint, the pace and the run of failures are recorded (04-profile) and change what is chosen next (section 3) — never the rating" (SPEC 2.2 standalone), and "mastered" is set only after an answer without a hint: "The hint would make the answer measure the hint" (SPEC 2.5 standalone; R187 standalone). The request and "explain differently" do not change the outcome in R82 spec either. A technique at a step is not help: the new profile goes "by steps" (concepts 10 spec), and in this mode "mastered" would otherwise never come.
- **A repeat of a task is help, not weight 0.** The solution goes to the device when the attempt is closed by a correct answer or by showing the solution (R82 spec; R09 api), so "the solution has already been seen" is almost any repeat of a solved task, not of a template, and a workbook page is never a template in v0 (concepts 9.3 spec). At weight 0 a repetition of a mastered skill on an exhausted pool would not refresh τ: the date would not move, and the engine would give the skill again and again. As help, a repeat refreshes τ but does not set "mastered" — the learner could remember the key: «ключ ученик знает» — "the learner knows the key" (R10 api).
- **The discount by attempt.** "Ten fractions in five minutes are not ten independent pieces of evidence" (concepts 11 spec). Models that count the answers of one stem as independent "will overestimate the precision with which examinee proficiency is measured", and this leads to "prematurely ending an examination" ([Bradlow, Wainer & Wang 1999](https://doi.org/10.1007/BF02294533), abstract) — here, an early "mastered". 1/(1 + (N − 1)ρ) is the share of the effective sample size at an intra-class correlation ρ: the effect of a cluster "depends on two factors: the subsample size within selected PSUs (b) and the intra-class correlation (ρ)" ([Kalton, Brick & Lê 2005](https://unstats.un.org/unsd/hhsurveys/pdf/chapter_6.pdf)). At ρ = 1/3 an attempt weighs no more than three answers, and a task of one item exactly one, so the module of olympiad maths counts as before.
- **The discount over the whole attempt, not over a set of skills.** Otherwise items with the skills {A} and {A, B} on one page would get no discount, and a page of four sets of five items would shift θ like 8.6 answers. The answers depend on the shared stem and one session, not on the set of skills.
- **One step per piece of evidence.** The order of a page's items then changes nothing, as with R in R04, and what is appended is counted by one function of the piece of evidence, which simplifies the condition of R80 spec.
- **"Mastered" by pieces of evidence.** Two answers "not yet" in a row by the order of places on the page would depend on where the author put the items, and scattered errors would never remove "mastered". A failure by the share of correct answers for a task of one item is exactly the rule of R03.
- **1/J of the step, and m in full.** P has the mean δ̄ (R03), and a shift of δ̄ by an answer's step is 1/J of the step to each of the J skills. m counts the answers where the skill is primary (R03): at 1/J, a skill that always stands in a pair would need twice as many tasks for "mastered".
- **Nothing to the supporting ones.** They are not in P (R03), and an answer tells the model nothing about them, while a step only at a correct answer would pull their estimate up without measure. An error does not lower them by concepts 6 spec either.
- **Outcomes by items.** Showing the solution is "not solved", as О-7 recommended and R82 spec decided, and "I don't know" in standalone is a wrong answer: "a child who has seen the solution has not solved the task" (R93 standalone). "Left unfinished" is not "not solved" (R82 spec), and what the learner did not answer the model does not judge, like a skip in standalone: "punishing that would punish a stray tap" (R98 standalone). "Unchecked" is not an error (R04 spec), but the verdicts before it were given by the program, and there is no reason to lose them: R82 spec rejected "unchecked" for a left attempt for exactly that reason, that at zero weight "the incorrect answers before leaving would be lost to the model".
- **τ by the share of correct answers.** At S̄ = 1 and 0 these are the rules of R04, and one miss on a page of ten does not halve τ, as an answer "not yet" would. There are no new constants.
- **Provenance.** With a key derived by AI there is no verdict, and a task from paper that AI itself tagged and solved "does not move the rating" (concepts 9.4 spec; О-11). An adult's key gives a verdict (О-11), but a task from paper has no band and difficulty on the module's scale, and without them P cannot be computed. R82 spec does not put an AI trap into the evidence, so its weight is 0, not "reduced", as concepts 9.4 spec writes.
- **A probe without a lift.** The lift of R299 standalone relied on the pause — a rule of choice —, and standalone recognized a probe by where the learner stood: "Whether a task is a probe is read off where the child stands when it is answered" (SPEC 2.2 standalone). Here the pause is a rule of `engine`, and the model would have to read its state. A correct answer at a low P raises δ strongly anyway.
- **No place for the rubric.** An attempt of a task with a rubric has no evidence (R80 spec; О-17): the weights need no place, and the weight of feedback will come with a new version together with the answer to О-17.

— *Rejected:*
- partial credit by the number of hints, for example S = 1/2^k: new constants without data, and the log carries k anyway — it can be introduced later;
- the outcome as a multiplier of the step at S, the item's result at the end of the attempt: a correct answer after three hints would go as correct, only weaker;
- an answer with help at weight 1/2: one more constant without data; a technique at a step as help too: in the mode "by steps" "mastered" would not come if the module's steps give a technique;
- a request for a technique as an error of the first attempt, like a request for a hint in KDD Cup 2010 ("an error (incorrect attempt or hint request)", [Data Format](https://pslcdatashop.web.cmu.edu/KDDCup/rules_data_format.jsp)): a technique names a way, not an answer, and R82 spec does not let the request into the outcome;
- a discount of √N — it grows without bound; a series as one answer with the share of correct ones — a page of twenty items would weigh like one task; a discount by an identical set of skills — above, "The discount over the whole attempt";
- a step for each answer in the order of the items, with "not yet" in a row at a total weight of 2: the order of places on the page would start to mean something; counters by pieces of evidence, with weight only in the step: a page of ten and one task would weigh the same for the uncertainty and "mastered";
- the whole step of δ to each primary skill: at J = 2 δ̄ would shift twice as much as one answer; m by 1/J — above;
- τ and the time of practice to the supporting ones at a correct answer: "time to review" would be put off by an answer that says nothing about that skill;
- not reading "unchecked" at all: the verdicts that the program has already given would be lost; showing without "not solved" for empty items: an adult who showed the solution at once would not move the model;
- a repeat of a task at weight 0 and at weight 1/2: above, "A repeat of a task is help";
- τ: all correct — it grows, otherwise by half — one miss out of ten would halve τ; a majority — a jump at the boundary;
- an adult's key at weight 0 until stage 5 — О-11 gives it a verdict; AI traps in the evidence at weight 1/2 — R82 spec does not put them there, and this would become a row to it;
- a lift on a probe by a mark "probe" in the evidence from `engine` — a field that R299 standalone rejected in the profile, and a reachability threshold in the model;
- a place in the weights for feedback by rubric at weight 0 — there is no evidence, and no place is needed.

— *Consequence:*
- the 2026-11-02 amendments to R03 and R04 — above, at the end of their entries;
- В9–В13 in table 3 of RUN.md, «в реестр spec»: the first verdicts of items in the evidence — to R82 spec and concepts 11 spec, for `api`; the AI trap — to concepts 9.4 spec; an adult's key — to О-11; the probe, the repeat of a task, "mastered" by pieces of evidence and frequent traps — for T01 engine; pages, help and repeats — for T01 and T02 sim;
- Д7 in table 4 of RUN.md — ρ = 1/3, the failure boundary ½, the rule of τ for a page and the full weight of an answer with help: there are no data behind them;
- [architecture.md](architecture.md) — sections 1, 2, 2.3–2.6, 3, 3.1–3.3 and 4; CLAUDE.md and README.md — the weights of evidence.

— RUN.md, T03; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 1, 2, 3.1–3.3 and 4.

— *Amendment 2026-11-02:* **the transfer by `retired_skills` inside the step** (R08). Each recorded primary skill x of an item goes to the skills map(x): to itself, if it is in `skills` of the current version and with the assessability `verdict`; to its `replaced_by` with `verdict`, if it is retired; to none in all other cases. J is the number of the item's recorded primary skills whose map(x) is not empty: without a major version this is the former J, and a skill that reached none takes no share. A skill s gets in an item the share |{x : s ∈ map(x)}| / J of the step of δ: each part of a split skill gets the former skill's share in full, and a merged skill the sum of the shares. N_s, u_s, m, the stability, the time of the last practice and the number of pieces of evidence are counted by the skills after the transfer, once per item: the skills merged in one item are one skill, and their count and practice are not doubled. An item's δ̄ is the mean over its J recorded skills, and each recorded skill's δ is the mean of the δ of its skills after the transfer; R̄ likewise. For an item without skills after the transfer, J = 0, δ̄ = 0, R̄ = 1 and w = 1: it moves only θ and n. This changes the "A proposal of the plan and of the execution" above: only J and δ̄ are now counted by the recorded IDs, while N and u for θ and the trial series do not depend on the skills anyway. The argument "1/J is the derivative by δ̄" does not hold for a split skill — the derivative by the δ of one of two parts is half as large, — and the share in full holds by another: the δ of each part goes exactly as the δ of the former skill, and the item's δ̄ does not change with a major version — also when the item has other primary skills. Without a major version everything is as before. The author's decision in planning T04. — R08; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.3 and 3.5.

— *Amendment 2026-11-07:* the rules of this entry entered the code with the tasks T16–T18. T16 is the weights of the series, the shares 1/J and the failure, T17 is τ by the share of correct answers, T18 is the reading of a piece of evidence. The author's decisions in planning T18:
- **Weight 0.** A piece of evidence with a key or skill tags from AI moves nothing, like a piece of evidence without answers: neither θ and δ, nor n and m, nor practice and τ, nor traps, nor "mastered", and it creates no state for the module. Rejected: moving only the rating — then tags from AI would decide whose skill was practised, and a skill would sometimes have practice without m.
- **Provenance "not specified" or unknown** weighs 0. Weight 1 is given only by a key from the author, a solver or an adult together with tags from the author. Refined by the author 2026-11-07, after the review of T18: tags from a solver or an adult weigh 1 too, since concepts 9.4 spec says to count an adult's key "when … the skills are not from AI". Rejected: weight 1 — a new provenance may turn out unreliable, like AI, and a field that changes the weight acts only in a version that knows it ([architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.5).
- **The outcome "with a sketch as a hint"** is help for all the answers of the attempt, like a technique on request. Rejected: "as after a hint" — a sketch at the start of the attempt would give "mastered" from the first submission.

A proposal of the plan, not the author's answer:
- **"Solution shown".** "Not solved", S = 0, belongs to an item without a first verdict that is not submitted or whose submissions are all "unreadable" with the reason `blank`. Only another "unreadable" or "unchecked" gives no answer: it is not an error. A submitted item without a verdict and without reasons gives no answer either.
- **A rung of the ladder** before the item's first verdict means its number of wrong answers before that submission is greater than zero.
- **A key from an adult** weighs 1 by the tags: `Append` requires the band and the difficulty anyway.
- **A trap** goes to the skills of its `skills` that have a state after the step, and without them, to the module. Rejected: creating a state for a skill for the sake of a trap — a skill has a state only with practice (the 2026-11-07 amendment to R04).

— RUN.md, T18; [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.3, "What stands since T18".

## Knowing an anchor skill

**R07. The learner knows an anchor skill when "mastered" is set on at least one skill that teaches it — in any module whose graph came, and on any band. "Does not know" is given only by the modules that the engine gets, and otherwise the anchor skill is "unknown". The model gives each state with a reason and the skills that teach it, and computes it at reading. Forgetting does not remove knowing, and skills with `rubric` and `none` have no state.** The author's answers of 2026-11-02 in planning T09 (RUN.md), all five by the plan's recommendations. The rule is [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.4.
- **What is decided:**
  - knows — when "mastered" is set on at least one skill that teaches the anchor skill, in any module and on any band. The threshold is the same as for "mastered" (R03), there is no own one; several skills and modules give "or". The model gives the mastery band and the highest band where the skill is taught, and knowing with regard to the band is for later;
  - "knows" goes by "mastered" in any module whose graph came at reading: a module of the log, connected or not, and a connected module without evidence. "Does not know" goes only by the engine's modules, connected and suitable for the learner (the 2026-11-02 amendment to R07 api). For this, reading needs one more input — the set of the engine's modules;
  - three states with a reason: "knows"; "does not know" — the skills with `verdict` that teach it are, in the engine's modules, not mastered or still without evidence; "unknown" — there is nobody to teach it, or only skills without a verdict teach it. With each state the model gives the skills that teach it, with their state. Whether to hold a lesson back at "no evidence" is decided by `engine`. The grade does not give knowing;
  - forgetting does not remove knowing: knowing is a function of "mastered", and the model gives the retention and the review date of the skill that teaches it together with it;
  - skills with `rubric` and `none` have no state. An anchor skill that in the engine's modules is taught only by such skills — or by skills with `verdict` that are primary in no band and therefore cannot be mastered — is "unknown" with the reason "only skills without a verdict teach it".
- **A proposal of the plan and of the execution, not the author's answer:**
  - the reasons: for "does not know", "not mastered" if a skill that teaches it has answers, and "no evidence" if none of them has; for "unknown", "nobody to teach it" and "only skills without a verdict teach it";
  - the model gives the skills that teach it by full ID, with the estimate, "mastered", the bands, the retention and the review date;
  - a module without a current version gives no graph (R08 api), and its "mastered" is not seen;
  - an anchor skill as a supporting skill of a task gets nothing (R06), and the model does not read it in the evidence;
  - the model does not read the module index (R63 spec).
- **What R07 does not decide:**
  - whether "mastered" carries over by `retired_skills`, and with it knowing after a major version of the module that teaches it, and how the input about the engine's modules fits into the type of reading — T04 and T05;
  - what the engine does at "no evidence", when knowing goes away and at a cycle of prerequisites between modules through anchor skills — `engine` (R50 spec);
  - knowing by skills with `rubric` — with the answer to О-17.

  What R07 hands them is [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 2.4 and 2.5.

— *Why:*
- **"Or" over the skills, the threshold of "mastered".** A skill that teaches an anchor skill teaches it whole: "a learner who has the skill can do what the description of each says" ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 12), and "to know a skill" in the model is exactly "mastered" (concepts 11 spec). A second threshold beside the threshold of "mastered" would be a second number in the same library, and R03 keeps one.
- **Any band.** The bands of different modules are not comparable, and the model's level is per module (R03), and the format does not say which band a module that rests on the anchor skill expects. In the first slice anchor skills are taught only by school maths of grade 3 (R50 spec), and it has one band.
- **"Does not know" only by the engine's modules.** A module that the engine does not get — disconnected or not suitable for the learner (the 2026-11-02 amendment to R07 api) — would hold the dependent skills back forever: the engine will give no sessions on it. `api` named it so too when it moved knowing into reading: for a disconnected module «его `teaches` считался бы во владении навыком-опорой» (RUN.md `api`, T04, `main` d896943, read 2026-11-02). And "mastered" in a disconnected module was derived by evidence, and it stays true: removing knowing by disconnecting would mean that the engine may offer again a module mastered to the end.
- **A reason for each state.** A grade 5 learner without school maths gets "unknown", and the lesson is not held back (concepts 4 spec), and after connecting it, "does not know": connecting a module would make the engine stricter. The reason "no evidence" leaves this case to the rule of `engine`, and the grade does not give knowing: "The grade decides one thing: where the child starts" (concepts 4 spec).
- **Without forgetting.** Time does not remove "mastered" (R04), nor knowing derived from it: otherwise after the holidays the skills of another module would close until a repetition in the first. The forgetting of a skill that teaches it is seen in its retention and review date.
- **No state for `rubric` and `none`.** They have no evidence: an attempt with a rubric does not give it (R80 spec; О-17), and `none` is only ever supporting, and an answer gives it nothing (R06). "Unknown" does not hold a lesson back, and in skills without a verdict the engine follows the author's order anyway (R58 spec).
- **At reading.** "Does not know" and "unknown" depend on the set of the engine's modules — on the connections and the profile — not on the log, and "knows" on the graphs that came at reading. They cannot be in the cache: so `api` decided (R08 api), and so the 2026-11-02 amendment to R80 spec says.

— *Rejected:*
- "and" over the skills that teach it — each teaches the anchor skill whole; an own cautious probability with an own threshold — a second threshold beside "mastered"; "mastered" only on the highest band where the skill is taught — the bands of modules are not comparable, and the format has no field "which band is needed";
- everything only by the engine's modules: after a mastered module is disconnected the anchor skill would become "unknown", and the engine could offer the module again; everything by the graphs that came, without a new input: a disconnected module with an unmastered skill would hold the dependent skills back forever;
- three states without a reason — with one more module the engine would become stricter; "unknown" while the skills that teach it have no answers — the lesson is open at first and closes with the first answers;
- knowing only while the review date of the skill that teaches it has not come: after a break the skills of another module would close until a repetition in the first;
- "does not know" for an anchor skill taught only by skills without a verdict — it would hold the dependent skills back always; a state with a time of practice for `rubric` and `none` — there is no evidence, and R06 rejected practice for a supporting skill;
- knowing in the cache — against the 2026-11-02 amendment to R80 spec;
- knowing by answers where the anchor skill is a supporting skill of the task: R06 rejected a step for supporting skills — a shift only upward at a correct answer would pull the estimate up without measure.

— *Consequence:*
- В14 and В15 in table 3 of RUN.md, «в реестр spec»: the set of the engine's modules in the input of reading — for `api`; three states with reasons, "unknown" at skills without a verdict and what the engine does — to concepts 4 and 11 spec and the card architecture 3.8 spec, for T01 engine and T01 sim;
- Д8 in table 4 of RUN.md — knowing without regard to the band;
- there are no amendments to R03, R04 and R06: R07 answers what they did not decide;
- [architecture.md](architecture.md) — sections 1, 2, 2.4–2.6, 3, 3.1, 3.3 and 3.4; CLAUDE.md and README.md — the anchor skill.

— RUN.md, T09; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 1, 2.6 and 3.4.

— *Amendment 2026-11-07:* reading and knowing an anchor skill entered the code with the task T20, and with them two marks of a module's trial series that R104 spec asks for (R15). The author's decisions in planning it:
- **the mark "the module's last piece of evidence was still in the series"** is a field of the module's state: the piece of evidence that ends the series clears its answers, and by n after it this can no longer be told. The mark is set only by a step that moved the module, and a piece of evidence at weight 0 or without answers, as before, changes nothing. Rejected: rewriting the mark by any piece of evidence of a module with a state — a piece of evidence at weight 0 would change the state for the first time, and in the first slice the weight is always 1 anyway;
- **an item's P in `Readout.Task` in the trial series is as at the step, R̄ = 1**: the P of reading is the P that the step would take for a piece of evidence at t₀. Rejected: always with retention at t₀ — in the series, reading and the step would diverge for skills practised long ago. This refines R04 too: [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.2, "At reading".

A proposal of the plan and of the execution, not the author's answer:
- **`Read`** takes the state, the graphs, t₀, the grade and the engine's modules. The modules of the output are the modules of the state and the engine's modules, by ID: a module with a state has the start band, both marks of the series and the skills; an engine's module without a state and with a current version has the starting point by grade, `BandByGrade`; and without a current version such a module is not in the output: it has no bands, and the engine will not get its graph. Each module must have an entry in the "graphs", and a graph is checked as in `Append`; otherwise, `ErrInvalid`. "The series has ended" is not stored in the state: it is n ≥ 5 − ε;
- **anchor skills** are each `core.<name>` that the graph of an engine's module with a current version names as a prerequisite or in `teaches`. The skills that teach them are taken from the modules of the state and the engine's modules with a current version: "mastered" exists only in a module with a state, so "any module whose graph came" is read without walking the map "graphs" (rule 6 of R09). A skill with `verdict` and non-empty bands where it is taught can be mastered. The teachers are all from these modules, by full ID byte by byte, with the flag `Practised`: a teacher without a state has only the ID and the highest band;
- **the mastery band and the highest band** are a `BandRef`, with the ID from the graph of the current version: the contract of the skill map names the mastery band by ID (R99 spec), and `api` does not look it up itself. A module without a current version has an empty ID;
- **the module's traps** are in the output of `Read` at the module, as a skill's traps are at the skill: a trap without skills with a state is counted only there, and which are frequent is decided by `engine` (R06);
- **`Readout`** holds a copy of the state, the graphs, t₀, the grade and the modules whose graphs `Read` checked, and `Readout.Task` computes by them without checking these graphs again. An item's P is computed by one function, `chanceOf`, both for the step and for `Task`, so the bits of the step have not changed. The step and reading take a new module by one function, `newModule`, with θ = θ₀: before, the step began a new module with θ = 0, but the series computes θ from θ₀ anyway, so the bits of the state have not changed, but the P of the first piece of evidence at the step is now the same as at reading. The provenance of the key and the tags does not change P: a piece of evidence at weight 0 moves nothing, but the probability of the answer is the same;
- `Append` and `Read` reject a state where the module is still in the series and the mark is not set: the step does not write such a state.

Checked 2026-11-07 in the container: `just ci-lint ci-test ci-proto ci-vuln` and `ci-licenses` are green — the vectors of each state and reason by the examples of section 3.4, the marks of the series before it, in it, at its last piece of evidence and after it, the example of section 3.2 through `Read` and `Readout.Task`, the equality of the P of reading and the P of the step bit for bit, the refusals, the property on random logs and the golden vectors of reading `testdata/read/` in all the runs of T13. — `readout.go`, `step.go`, `state.go`, `fold.go`; RUN.md, T20; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.1, 3.2, 3.4 and 3.7.

— *Amendment 2026-11-09:* an engine's module without a state whose current version has no bands `Read` gives without a starting point — a `ModuleReadout` with the ID alone, a zero `StartBand` and `HasEvidence: false` — and not `ErrInvalid` (RUN.md, T28; the debt Д15 is closed). The format lets a module without tasks have no `bands` ("A module with no tasks MAY leave `bands` out", [format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 11), and `NewGraph` accepts such a graph. Before, `Read` called `BandByGrade` on an empty list and failed for the whole learner. Why so: `engine` rejects an engine's module without the model's reading (`checkReadout`, `engine` at 5963f43, read 2026-11-09) and does not read the start band, so skipping the module would break the choice, while an empty start band would not; the skills of such a module still teach anchor skills, but without a band where they are taught they cannot be mastered — "unknown". Rejected: keeping the error; skipping the module, like a module without a current version; a `NewGraph` that rejects a graph without bands — then a module of lessons alone could not be given to the engine. `BandByGrade` still rejects an empty list: a piece of evidence without bands has no start band. Along with this, `checkGraph` rejects a skill taught on a band that the graph does not have: a graph without bands now reaches `Read`, and such a skill would otherwise pass for a skill that can be mastered. `Readout.Task` rejects a candidate of a module without a state and without bands with the name of the task and the module, not with a bare "no bands": the rule "no starting point" of `Read` and `Task` is one function, `startingPoint`. The reason of "unknown" `AnchorReasonNoVerdictTeacher` is "only skills that cannot be mastered teach it", without a verdict or without a band where they are taught, as in a module without tasks; the name is left from v0.1.0. A reader of `ModuleReadout` takes the start band only when its place is 1 or more. `sim`'s gate on this code is green, the numbers are the same (R20).

## Evidence and the log

**R08. A piece of evidence carries everything that the recomputation reads — the observations of the attempt, not the model's conclusions — and its form only grows: an earlier version reads a new form, skipping what it does not know. The state is a fold of the log: an append is the same step as the recomputation and gives the same bits on any build of one version of `learner-model`. There is no snapshot. The evidence of a retired skill goes to each of its replacements with the share in full, and the replacement derives "mastered" from the transferred evidence. The record of a choice also stores the grade, and a major version of a module does not change the places of the earlier bands.** The author's answers of 2026-11-02 in planning T04 (RUN.md), all seven by the plan's recommendations. How it is built is [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.5; the sources are section 4 there.
- **What is decided:**
  - "matches" in the condition of R80 spec is bit for bit. The state is a fold of the log in the order of `api`'s numbers: the recomputation is the fold from the empty state, an append is one of its steps over the cache. The bits are the same on any architecture, GOAMD64 level, processor and Go version for one version of `learner-model`;
  - there is no snapshot: `api`'s cache is the state at the end of the log, and a repeat of a choice recomputes the log up to the number that the choice stores;
  - the record of a choice also stores the grade by which the model computed the starting point of the modules without evidence — a row «в реестр spec» to architecture 1 spec;
  - the recomputation reads a band's place as recorded: the scale of bands is one for all versions of a module. A major version does not change the places of the earlier bands, and a new band stands only after the last one — a row «в реестр spec» to the rules of comparing versions;
  - the transfer by `retired_skills`: each replacement gets the retired skill's share in the step in full — for a split skill each part gets its whole history —, the skills merged in one item count as one skill, and only a skill with `verdict` serves as a replacement. A replacement's "mastered", stability and traps are derived from the transferred evidence by the same rules, and the flag "mastered" does not carry over separately. An item left without primary skills after the transfer moves only θ;
  - a piece of evidence carries everything that R82 spec names and everything that the model reads — as observations, not as the conclusions of R06. For an item: whether it was submitted, the reasons of its submissions without a verdict, the first verdict and the number of wrong answers before the submission that gave it, the verdict at the end and the number of wrong answers before the first "correct". Also the supporting skills, the band's ID beside its place and the places of the items for each trap. The piece of evidence does not carry the lesson's segment, the mode, AI's candidate traps or the work;
  - the form of a piece of evidence changes only by adding an optional field or a new value, and each version of the library reads a piece of evidence of any form: it skips an unknown field, counts an item with an unknown check kind or verdict as an item without an answer, and an unknown outcome as "unchecked".
- **A proposal of the plan and of the execution, not the author's answer:**
  - the step of an append reads only the graph of the module of its own piece of evidence. The input "graphs" names for the module of each appended piece of evidence — at the recomputation, all the modules of the log — the graph of the current version or "no current version", and a module of a piece of evidence without an entry is an error. A piece of evidence of a module without a current version moves nothing and waits, as R08 api decided;
  - the start band is proposed by the library's rule "band by grade" — by the grade from the profile and the bands of the version on which the attempt went —, and an append replaces it with the band from the state, if the module already has one, and gives the piece of evidence with the band it took. At the recomputation, too, the step takes the band from the state for a module with a state, and the band of the piece of evidence for a module without a state (the 2026-11-02 amendment to R03);
  - the details of the transfer: J goes by the recorded skills whose mapping is not empty, and N_s, u_s, m, the stability, the time of the last practice and the number of pieces of evidence by the skills after the transfer, once per item; δ̄ and R̄ are means over the recorded skills, and for each of them the mean over its replacements; for an item without skills after the transfer, δ̄ = 0, R̄ = 1 and w = 1; the skills of a trap go by the same mapping (the 2026-11-02 amendment to R06);
  - the thresholds n < 5 and m ≥ 5 are compared with a tolerance of 1e-9, and it is right while an attempt has no more than 170 items with an answer;
  - a field that a piece of evidence of an earlier form does not have is read as "not recorded", not as "no" and not as 0, and how to read it is decided by the version that added the field;
  - the time of practice is in whole seconds UTC;
  - the encoding of the evidence and the state is set by the library, canonically, and `api` stores the bytes as they are;
  - the model does not read anchor skills in the evidence, and it does not need the dictionary of `api`'s format window yet;
  - the model takes the module and the time of practice from the piece of evidence, and the columns of the same names serve `api` itself.
- **What R08 does not decide:**
  - how the code keeps the same bits, what types and encoding the library has — T05;
  - how the engine and `api` repeat a choice with an earlier version of `learner-model` — `engine`, its T03, and `api`;
  - the format of the export — the epic E4 of `spec` (R76 spec);
  - the fields of stage 5 — the provenance of the band and the difficulty of a task from paper (О-11).

  What R08 hands them is [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 2.4 and 2.5.

— *Why:*
- **Bit for bit.** The thresholds — "mastered", n < 5, m ≥ 5, failures in a row — make the state discontinuous: a difference in the last bit sets or does not set "mastered", and from there the states diverge forever. A tolerance on the numbers holds nothing here. A fold gives a match by construction if the step is deterministic and the cache is stored without loss. The step must be deterministic between builds too: while a rollout goes on, the earlier and the new image append under one mark (the 2026-11-02 amendments to R06 api), and the mark does not carry the build. Go itself does not promise the same bits: "An implementation may combine multiple floating-point operations into a single fused operation". The compiler fuses a multiplication with an addition on arm64 and on amd64 at GOAMD64 ≥ 3, and `math.Exp` on amd64 chooses its path by the processor right at run time ([architecture.md](architecture.md), section 4). But "An explicit floating-point type conversion rounds to the precision of the target type, preventing fusion that would discard that rounding": the same bits are reachable by the discipline of the code, and that is T05.
- **Without a snapshot.** The recomputation is needed only with a change of the cache's mark (R80 spec; R06 api), and a snapshot under the earlier mark is then of no use, and the transfer does not apply to it (the 2026-11-02 amendment to R80 spec). A snapshot for a repeat of a choice would be one more place where the state can diverge from the log. And the recomputation is linear in the log: a one-off Node script, a rough model of the step, recomputed 10,000 pieces of evidence in 13–18 ms.
- **The grade in the choice.** The inputs of reading are the cache, the graphs, the time, the grade and the engine's modules. The choice stores the digests of the graphs, by which the engine's modules are restored too (R08 api; В14 in table 3 of RUN.md), and the time (the 2026-11-02 amendment to R06 api), but not the grade. Without it the first choice in a module without evidence, whose starting point goes by grade (R03), will not repeat if the grade has changed since.
- **Bands as recorded.** The log is not rewritten (R80 spec), and the recomputation does not open packages of earlier versions (the 2026-11-02 amendment to R80 spec): a band's place is all it knows about it. While the places of the earlier bands do not change, the shift 2.5 · (p − 1) of the earlier pieces of evidence is the same (R03). The band's ID beside its place is for a table of correspondence, should one ever be introduced: it cannot be appended to the log after the fact.
- **The share in full.** The format says: "the evidence of a retired skill counts for each skill that replaced it" ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 12). With the share in full, the δ of each part of a split skill goes exactly as the δ of the former one, an item's δ̄ and R̄ do not change, even beside other primary skills, and a part's "mastered" comes from the same history. The skills merged in one item are one skill, as if they had been recorded so: otherwise the item's count and practice would double. A replacement without `verdict` has no state (R07). The flag "mastered" does not carry over separately, because it is a function of the evidence (R06), and the recomputation derives it anew, and with it knowing the anchor skill that the replacement teaches (R07). An item without skills moves θ: the level belongs to the module, and the task's band and difficulty remain, so the trial series does not change with a major version either.
- **Observations, not conclusions.** The log is not rewritten, and what a piece of evidence does not carry, a new version will no longer read from the old pieces of evidence. Observations — submissions, verdicts, the number of wrong answers — are read by the rule of R06 and later, perhaps, by partial credit (Д7 in table 4 of RUN.md), while a conclusion of R06, "blank" or "not solved", would be read only by R06. The list of R82 spec is the agreement by which `api` builds a piece of evidence. Beyond it and beyond what is read, a piece of evidence carries little for future versions: the IDs of bands, the supporting skills and the places of the items for traps — this is the module's content, not the learner's data —, and the number of wrong answers before the first "correct", without which partial credit cannot be introduced later. Nothing more: data at a minimum (R09 spec). The link of a lesson, which `api` asks about (R13 api), does not change the weights (R58 spec; R06), and it has no reader — so R82 spec refused to record what "has no reader until `learner-model` asks for it". The supporting skills are the module's content, not the learner's data, and the rule of the transfer of anchor skills (the 2026-11-02 amendment to R80 spec) presupposes them.
- **Reading a new form.** `api` rolls back only forward — a pull request with the earlier code (Т-19) —, and an earlier version of `learner-model` will recompute a log where pieces of evidence of the new form already are. The module index decides the same: a reader "reads these members of an index of any version by their places, not by the schema of its own version" (R63 spec). Standalone did not read a profile newer than itself: "an old instance rewriting a new file would quietly drop whatever it did not understand" (`04-profile.md` standalone). Here nobody rewrites the log, and reading it while skipping what is unknown loses nothing.

— *Rejected:*
- a match with a tolerance — the thresholds make the state discontinuous; bit for bit only on one build, with the build in the cache's mark — each change of the image would recompute all the logs, and a repeat of a choice on another architecture could diverge. This is the fallback path if T05 does not achieve the same bits, and then — as a row «в реестр spec» to R80 spec;
- a snapshot every N pieces of evidence — it is built anew with each change of the mark and can diverge from the log; a snapshot at each choice — as many copies of the state as there are choices (R09 spec);
- a repeat without the grade — the first choice in a new module will not repeat after a change of grade; the start band of the modules without evidence in the record of a choice — a number of the model in `api`'s record instead of the profile's data, one per module;
- a band's place without a row in `spec` — the comparison of versions would learn of the rule only when some module changes its scale; a band by ID, not by place — the format does not promise that a band's ID will not be taken for another one;
- equal parts between the replacements — the parts would start below the skill they came from and would need more answers before "mastered", though the learner learned nothing anew; the share in full but without "mastered", as R187 standalone reset the earlier "mastered" — knowing an anchor skill would go away too until new answers; the contribution of each recorded skill separately — for a merged skill the count and practice of one item would double; "as if recorded under the replacements" literally — each part of a split skill would get half;
- a piece of evidence with only what the model reads — the facts of R82 spec without weight would have to be removed from `spec`, and partial credit by old pieces of evidence could not be introduced; everything the attempt stores — the log would repeat the attempt, and there would be more data (R09 spec);
- a ban on rolling out an earlier version after a new form — `api` would stay on a version with a found error; refusing to compute such a log — the learner would have no model until a new rollout;
- a start band that `api` takes from reading at the start of an attempt, without replacing it at the append — the first two attempts of one module on two devices, with a change of grade between them, would carry different bands;
- the time of practice only as a column of `api` — a piece of evidence carries everything that the recomputation reads (the 2026-11-02 amendment to R80 spec).

— *Consequence:*
- the 2026-11-02 amendments to R03 and R06 — above, at the end of their entries. Т-22 does not come back: what is appended matches what is recomputed, as R80 spec says;
- В16–В19 in table 3 of RUN.md, «в реестр spec»: the form of a piece of evidence and the library's operations — for `api` and to the card architecture 3.8 spec; the grade in the record of a choice — to architecture 1 spec; the places of bands — to the rules of comparing versions; the form of a piece of evidence in the export — for the epic E4. В9 is "beyond", not "instead of";
- Д9 in table 4 of RUN.md — the tolerance 1e-9 at the thresholds of sums of weights;
- T05 — the same bits on any build, the canonical encoding, the rule of reading forms and the types of append and reading ([architecture.md](architecture.md), section 2.5);
- [architecture.md](architecture.md) — the status, sections 1, 2, 2.4, 2.5, 3, 3.1–3.5 and 4; CLAUDE.md and README.md — the evidence and the log.

— RUN.md, T04; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 2.4, 2.5, 3.5 and 4.

— *Amendment 2026-11-07:* the transfer by `retired_skills` entered the code with the task T19. The graphs with "no current version", the error for a module without an entry, the start band from the state and the tolerance at the thresholds stand since T16, and the reading of an unknown check kind, verdict and outcome since T16 and T18. The task required no decisions of the author. A proposal of the plan and of the execution, not the author's answer:
- **The transfer is one step.** `module-version.retired-kept` keeps `replaced_by` on the skills of the current version ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 27), and `NewGraph` keeps the same. So the graph of the current version names a chain of retirements already flat, and map(x) is one lookup, without recursion.
- **Without a transfer the bits are the earlier ones.**
  - The share of the step of δ is `w · (S − P) · k / J`, and the δ and R of a recorded skill are the mean over its replacements, in the order of IDs.
  - At k = 1 and one replacement, multiplication and division by 1 are exact, and the state without a transfer is bit for bit what T18 gave. The golden vectors of T16–T18 hold this.
- **Bit for bit with a log under the new IDs** the transfer matches when a split skill has two parts and no more than two skills merge in one item: (δ + δ) / 2 = δ and x · 2 / 2 = x exactly. With three parts or three merged skills, the mean and the share k/J may depart from such a log by the last bit. This does not touch the condition of R80 spec: the append and the recomputation go by one graph.
- **A trap's skills** go by the same mapping and without repeats: a trap two of whose skills merged into one is counted at it once. The rule of the 2026-11-07 amendment to R06 remains: a trap goes to the skills with a state after the step, and without them, to the module. A trap's skill that the graph does not know or that has become `rubric` goes to nobody, even if its state is left in the cache.
- **The graph of the transfer.** The step searches `Retired` by binary search and does not walk a chain. So `Append` rejects a graph where `Retired` is not in the order of IDs or has a repeat, where an entry's `replaced_by` is not in order, where a retired skill is still a skill of the graph or where a replacement is not a skill of the graph. A graph from `NewGraph` is never like that.
- **A cache with a skill that the graph has now retired** happens only if the graph changed under the same mark of the cache, while `api` recomputes with a new release (R80 spec; R06 api). `Append` does not touch or check such a skill.

— RUN.md, T19; [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.5, "What stands since T19".

## The build for the device

**R09. The library gives the same bits on any build of one of its versions — by the Go compiler, on any architecture, GOAMD64 level, processor and Go version, in WebAssembly too — and is built for the device as it is. This is held by the discipline of the code: the result of each multiplication and division of floating-point numbers at once passes an explicit `float64()`, and exp and log are its own, a port of pure Go from the package `math`. The library imports only Go's standard library, by a list. CI checks the same bits from the first code — by runs on amd64 at GOAMD64 v1 and v3, without processor extensions, on 386, in js/wasm and on arm64, and by a differential check of the assembler where the compiler fuses operations. By Т-10, compute on the server, and how it works offline is decided by stage 5.** The author's answers of 2026-11-02 in planning T05 (RUN.md) — seven: the first is to keep T05 one task, though the plan proposed to split it, the others by the plan's recommendations; those of them about the types and the encoding are R10. How it is built is [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.6; the sources are section 4 there.
- **What is decided:**
  - T05 is one task: the build, the same bits, the dependencies and CI are here, and the types and the encoding are R10;
  - the dependencies are only Go's standard library, by a list, both in the code and in the tests; `go.mod` without `require`. From `math`, only functions with an exactly defined result. exp and log are a port of pure Go from `math`, with `float64()` at each step, and the BSD-3-Clause notices of Go and Sun Microsystems are kept in the file of the port;
  - CI from the first code: tests on amd64 at GOAMD64 v1 and v3, at v1 with `GODEBUG=cpu.all=off`, at GOARCH=386 and in js/wasm through Node; a wasip1 build; a test of the rules of code on `go/types`; a differential check of the assembler — an ordinary build and a build with `-d=fmahash=n` on each architecture where the compiler fuses operations; a job of its own on `ubuntu-24.04-arm`; golden vectors of the state and of reading;
  - Т-10 — by a row «в реестр spec», to add to the recommendation, not to close it: compute on the server; `learner-model` keeps the build for the device and the same bits, and CI checks them from the first code; how it works offline — including the variant where the server chooses the queue in advance and the device only writes pieces of evidence — is decided by stage 5; `engine` follows the same discipline, its T05.
- **A proposal of the plan and of the execution, not the author's answer:**
  - the promise is for the compiler `gc`, on any architecture it knows, 32-bit too, at any GOAMD64 and GOARM64, on any processor and in any Go version not lower than the one `go.mod` names. TinyGo and gccgo are outside the promise. The assumption is the default floating-point environment: Go does not change it, but C code through cgo could;
  - the rules of code are eight, [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.6: `float64()` at each `*` and `/`; a list of functions of `math`; own exp, log, σ, power and retention in the public package `detmath`, so that `engine` takes the same port; neither NaN nor ∞; numbers into integers only with a range check, and what is stored and the time are `int64`; a canonical order without iterating over maps and with a byte-by-byte comparison of IDs; neither global mutable state nor goroutines; imports only from the list, without cgo, `.s`, `//go:linkname` and build tags;
  - a power with a fractional exponent has exact branches s = 0 and s = 1: at S̄ = 1 and 0 the rule of τ of [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.3, coincides exactly with the rules of section 3.2, while exp(1 · log a) need not coincide with a;
  - the list of the code's packages is [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.6; a new package goes into it by an amendment to this entry;
  - the differential check: at `-d=fmahash=n` all the fusion rules are off, so an identical assembler means the same as "nothing to fuse", and the build also prints "fmahash triggered" at each place where a rule would have fired. The control is `a*b + c`, `x/4 + y` and `c − a*b` in `testdata`;
  - the recipes `ci-test` and `ci-lint` are called by the shared wrapper `go.yml` from `.github`, and the arm64 job and the buf steps are own ones, in `ci.yml`, beside the shared ones;
  - the golden vectors are the state by the log, the output of reading and the table "input → bits" of exp, log, σ, power and retention; they change only with the version of `learner-model`;
  - the glue with the app — `syscall/js` or `//go:wasmexport` — goes in a separate package and only at stage 5.
- **What R09 does not decide:**
  - the types, the operations and the encoding — R10;
  - how it works offline — stage 5, by Т-10;
  - the discipline of the code of `engine` and the repeat of a choice on the device — T05 engine;
  - a run on targets that are not in CI — ppc64, ppc64le, s390x, riscv64, loong64, arm, mips and builds with software floating point — and the WASI environment — Д10 in table 4 of RUN.md.

— *Why:*
- **The same bits are reachable.** IEEE 754 addition, subtraction, multiplication, division and square root are correctly rounded, so each operation gives one result on any target. Go adds one liberty to this — "An implementation may combine multiple floating-point operations into a single fused operation, possibly across statements", — and "An explicit floating-point type conversion rounds to the precision of the target type, preventing fusion that would discard that rounding" (the Go 1.27 specification). The functions of `math` with their own assembly and the conversions that the specification leaves to the implementation also diverge. The rules of code close all three ([architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.6).
- **The experiment confirmed it** (a one-off program in the session's folder, 2026-11-02; RUN.md, T05). The port with `float64()` gave the same bits of exp, log, σ, power, retention and the likelihood of the trial series on a grid — on 114,561 and 200,009 inputs — in six builds: amd64 at GOAMD64 v1 and v3, v1 with `GODEBUG=cpu.all=off`, 386 and js/wasm. On 386 and in wasm the port equals `math.Exp` and `math.Log` bit for bit. But `math.Exp` on amd64 diverges from the port by up to 2 ulp on 18,069 inputs of 114,561, and with `cpu.all=off` on 15,562: the bits change with the processor even for one binary. The experiment's own harness, where `1 + r.Float64()*1000` was left without `float64()`, gave a different retention on v3 — rule 1 in action.
- **A differential check, not a list of instructions.** The rule `useFMA` stands at each fusion rule of each architecture, and `-d=fmahash=n` turns them all off (`ssa/func.go`, `base/hashdebug.go` of Go 1.27.3). A comparison of two builds does not depend on the names of instructions and will survive new rules and architectures. The experiment: the assembler of `detmath` matched on arm64, ppc64le, s390x, riscv64, loong64 and amd64 v3, and the control diverged on each.
- **The rule is for each `*` and `/`, not only for `a*b + c`.** The compiler turns a division by a constant with an exact reciprocal into a multiplication (`generic.rules`), so `x/4 + y` fuses too. Fusion goes across operators and through inlining — also into the code of a consumer that `api` builds with its own flags. This can be checked only in the library's sources, and a test on `go/types` checks the rule.
- **Own exp and log.** `math.Exp` has its own assembly on amd64, arm64, loong64, riscv64 and s390x, and on amd64 it chooses its path by the processor; `math.Log` on amd64 and s390x; `math.Pow` goes through them. The port takes the code that `math` itself runs on wasm and 386, and so matches it in wasm, and its accuracy is the same. Go's license is BSD-3-Clause, and Sun's notice permits copying "provided that this notice is preserved": both are compatible with Apache-2.0 (RULES.md, Licenses), and the license check does not see copied code — so the notices stand in the file, and the record is here.
- **Only the standard library.** The Go module of `spec` embeds the whole `format/` (`//go:embed all:format`), and it would get into each build, WASM too, while the library's types are its own (R28 spec). A third-party module is one more place where the bits or the bytes change with its version. Even `encoding/json` in Go 1.27 is built from v2, so the bytes of the stdlib encoders are not an agreement between Go versions.
- **CI from the first code.** Otherwise nobody would check R08's promise "on any build" until stage 5, and a violation would hide in the last bit until the first divergence of what is appended from what is recomputed during a rollout of `api`. A run on arm64 is free: GitHub's arm64 runners for public repositories are "free and unlimited", and `learner-model` is public.
- **Not closing Т-10.** The question is shared with `engine`, and T05 engine has not begun. And offline has a path that Т-10 does not name: the server chooses the queue in advance, and the device only writes pieces of evidence — then there is no computation on the device at all. The js/wasm build is useful anyway: it is one more compiler backend without fusion, and in it the port is checked against `math`.

— *Rejected:*
- splitting T05 into the build and the types with the encoding — a proposal of the plan by rule 11 of RUN.md; the author kept one task;
- `math.FMA` everywhere instead of separate operations — the same bits too, but on amd64 at v1 and in wasm it is in software and slow, and the differential check would then not tell an intended fusion from an accidental one;
- fixed point — rewriting the whole model, and own exp and log would be needed anyway; a correctly rounded libm — there is none in the standard library; tables of values;
- `-d=fmahash=n` in the consumer's build — a debugging flag of the compiler, and the library does not set the build of `api` and `sim`;
- the build in the cache's mark — the fallback path of R08, not needed: the same bits are reachable;
- third-party modules with a compatible license; the Go module of `spec`; the protobuf runtime — R10;
- checks of the device only from stage 5; a set without an arm64 runner; a minimum — the js/wasm build and the list of imports;
- closing Т-10 now; handing nothing «в реестр spec».

— *Consequence:*
- everything that R01, R03, R04, R06, R07 and R08 handed to T05 — the build, the same bits, the types, the encoding — is decided by this entry and R10; the earlier entries are not edited;
- В20 in table 3 of RUN.md, «в реестр spec»: to add to the recommendation of Т-10, and into the card architecture 3.8 spec, the same bits and only the standard library; for T05 engine, `sim` and `.github`;
- Д10 and Д11 in table 4 of RUN.md — the targets without a run and the runner's processor for v3;
- [architecture.md](architecture.md) — the status, sections 1, 2, 2.5, 3, 3.5, the new 3.6 and 4; CLAUDE.md and README.md — the standard library and the same bits.

— RUN.md, T05; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 2.5, 3.6 and 4.

— *Amendment 2026-11-07:* the checks of the same bits are in place (T13), and the code decided what the documents did not name. This is a proposal of the execution, not the author's answer:
- **The test of the rules holds more than the list of section 3.6.** Rule 1 — `float64()` at each `*` and `/`, neither `*=` and `/=`, nor `complex`, nor type parameters in arithmetic — it holds in the tests too: their inputs must be the same on all targets, and the experiment of T05 — `1 + r.Float64()*1000` in the harness without `float64()` and a different retention on v3 — is exactly about test code. The functions of `math` for the tests are the list of rule 2 plus `Exp` and `Log`, the reference of accuracy. In the code, but not in the tests, the test also holds rules 6 and 7: iterating over a map — also over a type parameter whose type set holds a map —, functions that read tables made from Unicode — for `strings` the tables of the package `unicode` (`ToLower`, `EqualFold`, `Fields`, `TrimSpace` and the like), for `strconv` its own tables of printable characters (`Quote`, `IsPrint` and the like) —, and the statement `go`. All this is caught by one pass over `go/types`, and it recognizes a function by its object, not by how it is written: `Exp` after `import . "math"` is `math.Exp` too. A type parameter in arithmetic — in `++` and `--` too. A file name that the command `go` reads as a build constraint is a build tag, as is a line `//+build` without a space; besides `.s`, no other sources that the command `go` would hand to something other than the Go compiler are allowed either — `.sx`, `.c`, `.syso` and the like.
- **The control of the test of the rules** is not a file but a module, `testdata/coderules`: a `require` in `go.mod`, a `.s` file and a constraint by name live outside a Go file. The test checks what it found against the `// want` comments of its lines in both directions and requires each rule at least once — so both a removed violation and a weakened check turn the check red. The external tests of a package see it together with its tests, and the test of the rules, like the command `go`, checks the other packages of the module that they import anew — against this package; the control holds this too: its package `sub` with tests of both kinds imports the control's root package, and that one imports `sub`.
- **Where the test of the rules runs.** The export data of the standard library is given to it by the command `go`, and that cannot be called from js/wasm: there the test is skipped, while on amd64, 386 and arm64 it runs. It takes no packages outside the list of the tests — `os/exec` and `go/build` are not in it.
- **The recipes.** `ci-test`, after the run with coverage, calls `test-targets` — the runs on targets — and `fma-diff` — the differential check. The arm64 job calls the same `ci-test`, and on a host that is not amd64 `test-targets` does not do the amd64 and 386 runs — a 386 binary cannot be run on arm64 —, and its own run goes with `GODEBUG=cpu.all=off`. wasip1 gets `go build` and `go vet`: so the tests for it at least pass type checking.
- **The differential check** builds the packages one at a time: the `-S` output of several packages comes in the order in which the compiler finished them, and two builds would diverge without fusion. It skips a package of tests alone — there is nothing to build in it —, and it prints a build that failed. Its output does not depend on the host, and the arm64 job repeats it in vain, for half a minute; this is the price of `ci-test` being one on all hosts, while the runner is free.

Checked 2026-11-07 in the container on amd64: `just ci-test` green, all the runs and `fma-diff` on six targets. The rules test is red if the violation is removed from the control — both with its comment and the whole line — and if the check is weakened; on `a*b + c` in the module's code both it and `fma-diff` on all six targets are red, and `fma-diff` also when the control is wrapped in `float64()`. — `rules_test.go`, `testdata/`, `justfile`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml`; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.6 and 4; RUN.md, T13.

— *Amendment 2026-11-07, second:* the package `detmath` is in place (T14), and the code decided what the documents did not name. This is a proposal of the execution, not the author's answer:
- **The bounds of the clamp** of rule 4: `Exp` — [−708; 708], where exp and σ are finite and normal on both sides: σ(−708) ≈ 3.3e-308, while at −709 and below 1 / (1 + e⁷⁰⁹) is already subnormal; `Log` — the positive normal numbers; for `Retention` — Δt in [0; the largest `float64`], so that a negative Δt — time that came backwards — gives 1, as does "Δt = max(0; …)" of section 3.2, τ in the positive normal numbers, so that 0 and a negative τ give a retention of almost 0, not 1, and F · Δt / τ no greater than the largest `float64`. The clamp sends NaN to the lower bound — in `Sigmoid` too, though −NaN does not change it: the function returns a number, not an error, and does not drop the step, though NaN never reaches it — the encoding rejects it.
- **`Pow` clamps the base and the exponent before the branches**: the base as `Log` does, the exponent to the finite numbers. a¹ = a for any a in the domain of `Log`, and 0¹ is 2⁻¹⁰²², not 0; s · ln a is never 0 · ∞, so 1ˢ = 1 for any s, ±∞ too. The bases of the τ rule — 1 + g · (1 − R) ≥ 1 and 1/2 — are far inside.
- **F is a constant of `detmath`, `RetentionFactor` = 19/81,** and C = 1/2 is the square root of the formula: retention is exactly these two constants, and `engine`, if it takes `Retention`, takes them with it. R(τ, τ) is 0.8999999999999999, not 0.9: 1 + 19/81 is not exact in `float64`.
- **A number to an integer in the port** is `truncate`, with a range check by rule 5: within ±2³⁰ — as `int(x)`, beyond them — saturation, NaN — 0. The port calls it with |x| < 1100.
- **The port's NaN** is the bits of `math.NaN`, `0x7FF8000000000001`, through `Float64frombits`: `math.NaN` is not on the list of rule 2, and with it the port equals `math.Log` bit for bit on a negative argument too.
- **The tests** compare the port with `math` bit for bit on 386 and in wasm, and on the other builds to within 2 ulp, except at the edges of overflow, NaN with a payload and subnormal arguments of `Log`, where `math`'s own assembly on amd64 departs from the correct answer and the port does not ([architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.6, "What experience showed"). There are two golden files: a table "input → bits" and the SHA-256 of the bits on 100,000 PCG inputs for each function; `DETMATH_UPDATE=1` writes them, not a flag: the package `flag` is not on the tests' list.

Checked 2026-11-07 in the container: `just ci-test` green — the tests of `detmath` in all the runs and `fma-diff` on six targets with the package `detmath` —, the coverage of `detmath` is 100% of statements; a removed `float64()` in the port of `log` turns the rules test, `fma-diff` and the golden files red at GOAMD64 v3. — `detmath/`; README.md; [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.6; RUN.md, T14.

— *Amendment 2026-11-07, third:* the control of the rules test, the module `testdata/coderules`, now keeps its `go.mod` under the name `go.mod.control`, and the rules test reads it under that name; the control's `go.sum` is deleted. A Go module zip carries no nested modules, and with `go.mod` in place the control would vanish for consumers, and `TestCodeRulesControl` would fail in their module cache.
