## Types and encoding

**R10. The library's types are its own and simple. The graph is per module: the bands in order and the skills with what the model reads, and also with prerequisites and tier for `engine`, while the bands where a skill is taught the library derives itself from the task descriptions. The task description is one for evidence and for a candidate task. Evidence and state are encoded in protobuf's binary form by a `.proto` schema of its own; the codec is its own, on the standard library, and buf checks the schema and the codec in CI.** The author's answers of 2026-11-02 in planning T05 (RUN.md): on the encoding and on the graph's types — both by the plan's recommendations. How it is built — [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.7; the sources — section 4 there.
- **What is decided:**
  - the graph carries what the model reads, and beyond that the prerequisites and the tier of a skill: the engine reads them for certain (architecture 3.8 spec). What else the rules of `engine` need, it will send as a row of its T04, while lessons and the pool are its types;
  - the encoding of evidence and state is protobuf's binary form. The codec is its own, on the standard library, without the protobuf runtime; the `.proto` schema is the description of the form; `buf lint`, `buf breaking` and `buf convert` are in CI.
- **A proposal of the plan and of the execution, not the author's answer:**
  - the packages — the module root `github.com/altnovatrail/learner-model` with the types and operations, `detmath` (R09), an internal codec, and the schema in `proto/novatrail/learnermodel/v1/`, package `novatrail.learnermodel.v1`;
  - the graph's fields — the module, the bands in order with IDs and grades; for a skill — the full ID, whether it is assessable, `teaches`, the bands where it is taught, prerequisites and tier, `retired_skills`; the input "graphs" — module → graph or "no current version" (R08);
  - the bands where a skill is taught are derived by a function of the library that builds the graph from the module's skills and the descriptions of its tasks: one rule, not a copy in `api` and another in `sim`;
  - the task description — the ID of the task or template, the band by place and ID, the difficulty, the primary and supporting skills, the provenance of the key and of the tags, the items with their skills and answer slots;
  - the operations of R08 — named `BandByGrade`, `Append`, `Recompute`, `Read`, `Readout.Task` and the codec; a grade has a separate value "no grade"; errors are values, without panics;
  - the rules of the encoding: `optional` on every scalar field, `double` — eight IEEE bytes in little-endian, the writer writes fields by number, `packed`, and the elements of repeated fields in canonical order, no `map<>` fields, the reader skips an unknown number by its wire type and rejects what is corrupt, a field number is never reused;
  - the schema follows the rules of `spec`'s contracts (R83 spec): the package `novatrail.learnermodel.v1`, names are terms in snake_case, an enum's zero value is `<NAME>_UNSPECIFIED`, `buf lint` — the categories `STANDARD` and `COMMENTS` with `disallow_comment_ignores: true`, and the field names in JSON are as in the schema;
  - `buf breaking` — against the previous tag, category `FILE`; `buf convert` of the golden bytes to JSON checks the codec against the schema.
- **What R10 does not decide:**
  - what else the rules of `engine` need in the graph's types — T04 engine, as a row to `learner-model`;
  - the export format — epic E4 of `spec` (В19 in table 3 of RUN.md);
  - the fields of stage 5 — the provenance of the band and the difficulty of a task from paper (О-11).

— *Why:*
- **Prerequisites and tier at once.** The card names prerequisites for the engine (architecture 3.8 spec), and section 2.4 of `engine` lists for a skill its tier, prerequisites and whether it is assessable (RUN.md and architecture.md of `engine`, `main` 08bc48a, read 2026-11-02). T04 engine has not started, and without these fields `api` and `sim` would build the graph twice — now and after it. The rest — names, descriptions, lessons — the model's rules do not need, and the card keeps the types simple, not a copy of the format.
- **The bands where a skill is taught come from the library.** It is a rule of the model (R03): the band of a task where the skill is primary for the task or the item. Were `api` and `sim` to derive it separately, the graph of one module could come in two forms — and R28 spec gave the types to one library precisely so that the graph is translated into one form.
- **Protobuf's binary form.** Numbers are eight IEEE bytes as they are, so −0 and the last bit survive the encoding with no rules about text. An unknown field is skipped by its wire type — R08's rule of reading holds for free. A `.proto` schema with comments in English is an open description of the form, which the export awaits (В19), and buf is already in `spec`'s container (R83 spec): `buf breaking` holds "the form only grows", and `buf convert` gives JSON of the same value. Experience: `buf convert` turned a hand-encoded piece of evidence into JSON — `"x":-0` — and back into the same bytes, and `buf breaking` caught a deleted field: "Previously present field "3" with name "x" on message "Evidence" was deleted".
- **Its own codec.** The protobuf runtime for Go is a third-party module (R09), and its deterministic output is promised only within one binary: "It is not guaranteed to remain stable over time" (`google.golang.org/protobuf`, `MarshalOptions.Deterministic`). The recomputation, however, compares the state's bytes between builds.
- **`optional`.** Without it a scalar field in proto3 has no presence, and "not written" would read as 0, while R08 tells them apart.
- **As `spec`'s contracts.** R83 spec has already set the platform's rules for protobuf — the packages `novatrail.<part>.v1`, `STANDARD` and `COMMENTS`, "A field's name in JSON is its proto name, snake_case" —, and `learner-model`'s schema follows them, starting no second style beside the contracts, while the export's JSON (В19) writes the same names. `buf convert` can do so: `#format=json,use_proto_names=true` gave `"practice_time"` and `"first_verdict"` (experience of T05).

— *Rejected:*
- only what the model reads in the graph: prerequisites and tier would come in a new version after T04 engine; the whole server part of the graph — the card keeps the types simple;
- the bands where a skill is taught as a field that `api` and `sim` fill by their own copy of the rule;
- JSON by RFC 8785 — readable without tools, but numbers are text through `strconv`, several times more bytes, and the rule "only grows" would be checked by a test of its own; a binary form of its own — neither tools nor a standard, and the export would need its own text about the bytes;
- the protobuf runtime and generated code — above, "Its own codec";
- proto3 without `optional` — "not written" is indistinguishable from 0; a package of its own `learnermodel.v1` and `STANDARD` alone — a second style of protobuf beside `spec`'s contracts;
- the elements of repeated fields in the order of iterating maps — one state would give different bytes.

— *Consequence:*
- В21 in table 3 of RUN.md, «в реестр spec»: the graph's types and the task description — to the card architecture 3.8 spec, for T04 engine, `api` and `sim`. В16 and В19 are refined: the operations are named, the description of the form is the `.proto` schema;
- [architecture.md](architecture.md) — sections 2.5, 3.5, the new 3.7 and 4; CLAUDE.md and README.md — the encoding.

— RUN.md, T05; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 2.5, 3.7 and 4.

— *Amendment 2026-11-07:* the types, the schema and the codec are in place (T15). The author's answers in planning T15 are three: T15 is one task, though it is bigger than one check; the Go API is values, without pointers, and a field that may be absent by its meaning is a value with a flag beside it; `buf breaking` checks against nothing before the first tag, and from the first one against the latest tag `v*`, category `FILE` (R83 spec does the same at the first release with a package). The rest is a proposal of the execution, not the author's answer:
- **Values and flags.** The state is copied by assignment, and an append shares nothing with the state it started from — with pointers it would have to copy deeply. Flags are where a field is absent by its meaning: the grade (`Grade.Known`), the mastery band (`Mastered`), the hint's rung for the outcome "after a hint", the number of options for a choice, the fact "the solution went to the device" — a template has none —, the first and the last verdict and the item's first "correct". The schema holds the same by the presence of `optional`: a field without a value is not written.
- **The schema** is three files in `proto/novatrail/learnermodel/v1/`: `common.proto` — `BandRef`, `AnswerSlot`, `TrapCount` and the enums `CheckKind`, `Verdict`, `Provenance`; `evidence.proto` — `Evidence`, `ItemEvidence`, `FoundTrap`, `Outcome`, `UnverdictedReason`; `state.proto` — `State`, `ModuleState`, `TrialAnswer`, `SkillState`. The numbers follow the order of the tables of section 3.5 and the state's list of section 3.7, `kind` first. `CheckKind` holds only the kinds with a verdict: an attempt of a task with a rubric gives no evidence (О-17), and a new kind is an added value. A template task is the flag `template`, an item with its own skills is the flag `own_skills`: an empty list of its own primary skills could not otherwise be told from their absence.
- **The Go types.** The task description `Task` is one for evidence and for the engine's candidate, while what happened to an item in the attempt is `Evidence.Items`, by place; the codec joins them into one `ItemEvidence` and rejects places that do not match one to one. The input "graphs" is `Graphs`, a map module → `ModuleGraph{Graph, Current}`: the model only reads it by key and does not iterate it (rule 6). The enums are types of their own over `int32` with the schema's numbers, and a value this version does not know stays a number. The types of the reading's output are without the computation, the computation is T20. `NewGraph` derives the bands where a skill is taught — the bands of the tasks where it is primary for an item: by the item's own skills, or by the task's skills for an item without its own and for a task without items; a skill of a task whose every item names its own skills is trained by no item, and that task's band does not count for it. It rejects a duplicate ID and a duplicate within one list — of replacements, anchor skills, prerequisites, grades —, a band's place out of order, a task's band outside the bands or with the ID of another band in its place, a difficulty outside 1–5, a primary skill and a replacement that the module does not have.
- **Errors** are values, of three kinds, which `errors.Is` tells apart: `ErrInvalid`, `ErrCorrupt` and `ErrNotFinite`; the message names what and where. There is no `fmt` in the code — it is not on the list of rule 8.
- **A strict writer.** It rejects a kind outside 1…`EvidenceKind` or 1…`StateKind`, a string not in UTF-8 — a proto3 `string` field must be UTF-8 —, a duplicate place, skill, trap or module, and a value without its flag: a rung without `HasRung`, a number of wrong answers without a first verdict and the like. Otherwise the value would silently vanish from the log, which is never rewritten.
- **A strict reader.** Besides skipping an unknown field by its wire type — a 32-bit one too, though the schema takes none —, the reader rejects: a field of the wrong wire type, a non-repeated field twice, a field that every kind from 1 on writes, if it is missing, a value without its flag's field, kind 0, a string not in UTF-8, the elements of a repeated field not in canonical order or twice — except the answer slots, the reasons without a verdict and the answers of the trial series, whose order is the order of events —, a `bool` other than 0 and 1, an `int32` out of range, groups, field number 0, NaN and ∞. Protobuf reads a repeated field as "the last one wins", but the writer never writes so, and a repeat means corruption. What the reader accepted, the writer returns as the same bytes, except unknown fields. A varint not in its shortest form and fields not in the order of their numbers the reader accepts: the value does not change by it. A kind newer than its own it reads. The wire type is checked in one place, before the value is read, and one generic helper reads the values.
- **buf** — `buf.yaml` v2 at the root, the module `proto`, without `deps`, as in `spec`; the recipe `ci-proto` and the job "Proto" in `ci.yml`, with tags in the checkout for `buf breaking`. The latest tag is by version, with `versionsort.suffix=-`, so that a pre-release `v1.0.0-rc.1` comes before `v1.0.0`, not after. The golden bytes `testdata/wire/*.binpb` and their JSON beside them: `buf convert` turns the bytes into this JSON and the JSON back into the same bytes. `just proto-json` writes the JSON, on one line, as buf gives it.

Checked 2026-11-07 in the container: `just ci-lint ci-test ci-proto` green — the codec there and back on random values, the golden bytes in all the runs of T13, rejection of every field of every message written twice or with the wrong wire type, of a value without its flag, of a string not in UTF-8 and of elements not in canonical order; `buf lint` is red on a field without a comment, `ci-proto` on a JSON that did not match, and `buf breaking` against a tag in a temporary repository catches a deleted field: "Previously present field "9" with name "failures_in_a_row" on message "SkillState" was deleted". The coverage of the code is 98.2% of statements. — `types.go`, `evidence.go`, `state.go`, `readout.go`, `errors.go`, `graph.go`, `codec.go`, `codec_check.go`, `codec_read.go`, `internal/wire/`, `proto/`, `buf.yaml`, `testdata/wire/`, `justfile`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml`; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.6 and 3.7; RUN.md, T15.

— *Amendment 2026-11-07, second:* T20 added to the schema and the types what the reading needs (the 2026-11-07 amendment to R07). A proposal of the plan and of the execution, not the author's answer:
- **field 8 of `ModuleState`** — `optional bool last_evidence_in_trial`, `LastInTrial` in Go: whether the last piece of evidence that moved the module was still in the trial series. It is always written, and the reader requires it, as it does fields 1–4. The state kind stays 1: there has been no release, and there are no states outside the repository. The golden bytes `testdata/fold/` and `testdata/wire/` are written anew, and the difference is exactly field 8: a one-off test decoded the old and the new states and compared them without it;
- **the types of the reading's output**: the mastery band is `BandRef`, not a place; for a module — `TrialOver`, `LastInTrial` and the module's traps `Traps`; for a teacher — `Practised` and the highest band `BandRef`; `Readout` with unexported fields for `Task`. The signature is `Read(s State, g Graphs, at int64, grade Grade, engineModules []string) (Readout, error)` and `(Readout) Task(Task) (TaskReadout, error)`;
- one function, `checkTaskContent`, checks the task description of the evidence and of the candidate.

— *Rejected:* without a new field — keeping the series' answers until the next piece of evidence: those are answers after the end of the series, which `Append` rejects, and the meaning of field 5 would become double; storing n up to the last piece of evidence — a number for the sake of one bit. — `state.proto`, `codec.go`, `codec_read.go`, `readout.go`; RUN.md, T20.

- **What the tag brings `learner-model`** (`git -C spec diff v0.6.6 v0.9.3`, read 2026-11-06; Д82, Д86 and Д87 in table 4 of `spec`'s RUN.md):

The level is per module, guessing belongs to the item, by its answer slots: so say the card architecture 3.8 spec and concepts 11 and 15 spec, but without the number of guessing in concepts 11 spec — the numbers stay `learner-model`'s. The threshold "mastered" is a constant of `learner-model`, "mastered" with the mastery band is in the skill's state, the return to rotation is a rule of `engine`, and a new target of the corridor is a new constant of the model, in one run of `sim`: the 2026-11-02 amendment to R23 spec, concepts 11, 12 and 15 spec, the card and the addition to Т-16. It is a skill's retention that fades, not the estimate itself, and the skill's state holds the stability (concepts 11, 12 and 15 spec);

With format 0.3 this is the comparison rule `module-version.bands-kept` ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 27). Whether the weight needs the fact "segment of a lesson" it left to `learner-model`, and R08 has already given the answer: the evidence does not carry the segment, and it changes no weights;

## Code before the gate of `sim`

**R12. The model's first version is the baseline: its code and CI come before the first tag of `sim`, and with `spec`'s answer to В24 the first signed release does too, checked by its own tests, by the property "appended equals recomputed" and by standalone's test vectors. The gate of `sim` holds every change of the model after it.** The author's answer of 2026-11-06 in planning E2 (RUN.md, «Решено автором» of table 1).
- **Why.** The harness `sim` imports `learner-model` together with `engine` and takes its version by `go.mod`, while the library's CI takes `sim` by tag and puts in the pull request's code through `go.work` (repositories 1 spec; Т-16). Row 4.6 of `spec`'s `COORDINATION.md` puts the model's code after the first tag of `sim`, row 4.5, — but without a version of the model the harness has nothing to run, and a circle results. The criterion is written before the first run of a candidate (R163 standalone; concepts 11 spec), not before the first line of code: the baseline is what the harness compares the first candidate with. standalone's model, which it starts from, passed its own harness, and its numbers — Д5–Д7 — are what the gate will check first.
- **What this means for E2:** the scaffold, CI, `detmath`, the types, the codec and the model are tasks T12–T20, the first release v0.1.0 is T21, the gate is T22, when the first tag of `sim` comes out. Intermediate versions are not released. A release is immutable, and `spec`'s plan — row 4.6 of `COORDINATION.md` and repositories 4 spec — puts the model's code after the first tag of `sim`, so T21 waits for `spec`'s answer to В24, and T12–T20 do not: nobody takes code without a release.
- **What is outside the repository:** row 4.6 of `COORDINATION.md` is split — the first version after 4.1, without 4.4 and 4.5, the changes after 4.5 — and `sim` puts the harness on this version. The types are by R10, without waiting for T04 engine: before v1.0.0 the library's API may change. This is row В24 of table 3 of RUN.md, «в реестр spec»; if `spec` decides otherwise, R12 is revised.

— *Rejected:*
- only what does not change the model — the scaffold, CI, `detmath`, the types and the codec — before the tag of `sim`, and the model after: the circle remains, and the epic stops halfway;
- the whole epic waits for `sim`: the same, and longer still;
- `sim` keeps a copy of standalone's model as the baseline: the model would live in two repositories, and the harness would measure `learner-model` by someone else's code.

— *Consequence:* RUN.md — E2 in table 1, tasks T12–T23, В24 in table 3; CLAUDE.md and README.md — when the code comes; [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 1.

## Scaffold and CI

**R13. The library has a Go module `github.com/altnovatrail/learner-model` with `go 1.27.3` and no `require`, and CI checks for its code: the recipes `ci-lint`, `ci-test`, `ci-vuln` and `ci-licenses` in the `justfile` are called by the job "Go" — the shared `go.yml` of `.github` v0.1.15 —, the coverage goes to Codecov, and CodeQL reads Go. All the references to shared workflows are to v0.1.15. `ci-lint` is `gofmt`, `go vet` and the check of the gopls version (R14), with no other linters.** The author's answers of 2026-11-06 in planning T12 (RUN.md, T12, «Решено автором») — by the plan's recommendations.
- **What is set up:**
  - `go.mod` — `go 1.27.3`, the Go version of `spec`'s container: R09's promise holds "in any Go version no lower than the one `go.mod` names", and it is checked on 1.27.3. There is no `require` and will be none (R09). At the root — `doc.go` of the package `learnermodel`, in English: what this library is;
  - the `justfile` — after the pattern of `module-kit` (R04, R18 module-kit), without its recipes of the release and of the check of `spec`'s modules:
    - `ci-lint` — `gofmt` over the Go files git knows, except `testdata/`, `go vet ./...` and the check of the gopls version (R14);
    - `ci-test` — `go test -race -count=1 -covermode=atomic -coverpkg=./... -coverprofile=coverage/go.out ./...`: one Go profile, which the wrapper's job `coverage` sends to Codecov by OIDC, without a secret (R48 spec). T13 will add the runs on the targets of R09 here;
    - `ci-vuln` — govulncheck v1.9.0, `ci-licenses` — go-licenses v2.0.1 with the list `ALLOWED_LICENSES`, which the wrapper passes. Both are `go run` at an exact version, without installing, outside `go.mod` and outside Dependabot: they are raised by hand;
  - `.github/workflows/ci.yml` — the job "Go" by the text of architecture 3.3 .github, with `contents: read` and `id-token: write`; `codeql.yml` — `languages: go`; `.github/codecov.yml` — the template's text, as in `module-kit`: the changed lines covered no less than 90%; `dependabot.yml` — the ecosystem `gomod` by the template, though it has nothing to raise; `.gitignore` — `coverage/` and `dist/`;
  - the references to `secrets.yml`, `dco.yml`, `documents.yml`, `codeql.yml` and `scorecard.yml` — from v0.1.0 to v0.1.15, commit 89d3cee, together with `go.yml`;
  - README — the Go version badge (repositories 2.3 spec). There is no coverage badge: the profile has no line yet, and a badge without data is not put up (R48 spec). The task after whose merge the upload on `main` first carries lines puts it up.
- **Checked** (2026-11-06): in the container `just ci-lint ci-test ci-vuln` and `ci-licenses` with the list of `go.yml` pass; the profile is one line `mode: atomic`; govulncheck — "No vulnerabilities found"; `ci-licenses` without the list fails and says where to get it; `ci-lint` fails on a file that `gofmt` would rewrite. `gh release verify v0.1.15 -R altnovatrail/.github` — "Release v0.1.15 verified!". The run of the job on a pull request — after the author's push.

— *Why:* without the module, the recipes and the job "Go" the code of T13–T20 has nowhere to pass its checks, while tests and `codecov/patch` come with every change of code (RULES.md, How we work; R48 spec). What the code adds to the files of CI is written by the repository's own session (the 2026-10-21 amendment to R30 spec). The references are all to v0.1.15: `codeql.yml` takes `go` only in the new releases, `go.yml` with the coverage upload is from v0.1.12, while `secrets.yml`, `dco.yml`, `documents.yml` and `scorecard.yml` did not change between v0.1.0 and v0.1.15, so raising them changes one SHA, and Dependabot's pull requests #5–#9 will close by themselves. Codecov does not need turning on: its app is installed in the organization for all repositories, and it turns the repository on with the first upload ([architecture.md](architecture.md), section 4). It costs no money: the repository is public (R30, R48 spec).

— *Rejected:*
- raising only `go.yml` and `codeql.yml` by hand, as R18 module-kit did: the files would hold different releases, and #5–#9 would have to be merged or closed separately;
- staticcheck in `ci-lint`: the rules test of T13 catches R09's rules, SonarCloud the code smells, and one more tool outside Dependabot would have to be raised by hand with every Go version;
- a `go` line below 1.27.3, as `go 1.27.0` in `module-kit`: R09's promise would name versions on which nobody checked it;
- the coverage badge at once: before the first line of code it would show "unknown".

— *Consequence:* the author's step — in the ruleset `main`, "Go / lint", "Go / test", "Go / vuln", "Go / licenses" and "CodeQL / analyze (go)" with `integration_id` 15368 after the run of T12's pull request. `codecov/patch` with `integration_id` 254 is put by the executor, on the author's instruction of 2026-11-06, — after the first pull request of Dependabot with an upload, as the first step of the task before which this condition is met (the 2026-10-23 amendment to R30 spec; architecture 3.3 .github; RUN.md, T13). Not earlier: a required check that never comes would hold the merge of every pull request, and the ruleset's bypass list is empty. — `go.mod`, `doc.go`, `justfile`, `.gitignore`, `.github/`; README.md; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.6 and 4; RUN.md, T12.

**R14. gopls stands in the devcontainer image at an exact version, v0.24.0, and is built from its own lock file `.devcontainer/gopls/`, as in R19 module-kit. This is a difference of `learner-model`'s container from `spec`'s container until `spec`'s template gives gopls to the kind `go`.** The author's answer of 2026-11-06 in planning T12 (RUN.md, T12, «Решено автором»).
- **What is set up** — the same as in `module-kit`, word for word, except the repository's name and the entry's number:
  - `.devcontainer/gopls/go.mod` — the module `devcontainer/gopls` with `tool golang.org/x/tools/gopls`, and `go.sum` holds the hash of every module of the build. `go mod init` and `go get -tool golang.org/x/tools/gopls@v0.24.0` gave them; `go.sum` matched `module-kit`'s `go.sum` byte for byte, and `go mod verify` — "all modules verified";
  - in the stage `devcontainer` of the Dockerfile — `ARG GOPLS_VERSION=0.24.0`, a `COPY` of the lock file and `go install -mod=readonly tool` into `/usr/local/bin`; `gopls version` must name `GOPLS_VERSION` as a whole line. The stage `toolchain`, in which the checks run, does not change;
  - `devcontainer.json` — the Go extension does not update its tools, does not look for their updates and does not lint on save, and the comment to `context` says what the image takes from its folder `gopls/`. A missing tool — the debugger dlv, say — it still offers to install, without an exact version, and such an offer is declined;
  - `ci-lint` checks `GOPLS_VERSION` against the version in the lock file: CI does not build the stage `devcontainer`;
  - `dependabot.yml` does not take the folder `.devcontainer/gopls/`: gopls is raised by `go get -tool` in it together with `GOPLS_VERSION`.
- **Checked** (2026-11-06): the lock file in a temporary folder builds gopls by the same `go install -mod=readonly tool`, and `gopls version` is `golang.org/x/tools/gopls v0.24.0`; `ci-lint` fails and names both versions with `GOPLS_VERSION=0.24.1` and without `ARG GOPLS_VERSION`. There is no Docker in the container, and the image was not built here: the author's Rebuild Container will check it.

— *Why:* without gopls in the image the Go extension installs its latest version by itself, not an exact one (RULES.md, Exact versions only). `spec` has already decided to give the kind `go` the same step (R102 spec, the answer to В19 module-kit), but the epic E7 that will do it came out neither in v0.9.3 nor in v0.9.4 (`gh api repos/altnovatrail/spec/compare/v0.9.3...v0.9.4`, 2026-11-06). gopls v0.24.0 is the latest, of 2026-10-14 ([architecture.md](architecture.md), section 4); the license is BSD-3-Clause, and it is a development tool: it is shipped nowhere and puts none of its own code into what ships.

— *Rejected:*
- waiting for `spec`'s template: until the tag with E7 the Go extension would install gopls at an unpinned version;
- only forbidding the extension to install tools: no language server until the same tag.

— *Consequence:* the Dockerfile and `devcontainer.json` no longer match `spec`'s recipe byte for byte. The move to the tag of `spec` where the template gives gopls to the kind `go` removes the step of its own and replaces this entry — Д12 in table 4 of RUN.md. Rebuild Container is the author's step. — `.devcontainer/`, `justfile` (`ci-lint`), `.github/dependabot.yml`; CLAUDE.md; RUN.md, T12, table 4.

— *Superseded 2026-11-09:* by entry R19 — since `spec` v0.9.16 the step of gopls and the lock file are given to the kind `go` by `spec`'s template.

## The `spec` tag v0.9.5

**R15. `learner-model` stands on `spec` v0.9.5 — the latest release. The tag records `spec`'s answers to both rows «в реестр spec», В23 and В24, by their recommendations, and the rows are closed. It also asks of `Read` two signs of the module's trial series (R104 spec), and that goes into T20.** The author's decision of 2026-11-07 — move to the latest tag; a task outside an epic, T24 of RUN.md.
- **What the tag brings `learner-model`** (`git -C spec diff v0.9.3 v0.9.5`, read 2026-11-07; Д111 and Д112 in table 4 of `spec`'s RUN.md):
  - the answer to В24 (Д111) — R103 spec, which closes Т-16 too:
    - the model's first version is the baseline before the first tag of `sim`, released signed. R103 spec split row 4.6 of `COORDINATION.md` into 4.6–4.8;
    - the gate: the library's CI takes `sim` by its exact tag and the SHA of its commit, in a job of its own on `ubuntu-24.04` with `timeout-minutes: 15`, in the stage `toolchain` of the devcontainer of `sim` at the same tag. It writes `go.work` on the fly, without committing it: `sim`, the pull request's code and `engine` — by the commit from the gate file of `sim`. The gate runs on every pull request and every commit of `main` and holds the merge;
    - the order of releases: a change that breaks the harness's API or moves the numbers is first recorded in `sim` at the commit of its pull request; a tag of `sim` comes out; the model's pull request raises it and is merged; after the model's release `sim` raises `go.mod`. The pair of the threshold "mastered" and the target of the corridor, and a change of the model that breaks the API of `engine`, go in one tag of `sim`: the gate file names both commits, the model is merged when the pull request of `engine` has passed review, and `api` moves to both versions together.

    This is the 2026-11-07 amendment to R12, T21 and T22;
  - the answer to В23 — the 2026-11-07 addition to О-25, by option 1. An answer in the second way is evidence with the weight of a handwritten answer; a submission whose verdict the learner saw the model always counts; one cancelled before the verdict does not reach evidence. The card architecture 3.8 spec says the same. It needs no fields of evidence and no code — subsections 2.7 and 3.3 of [architecture.md](architecture.md);
  - R104 spec (Д112): `Read` gives for a module whether its trial series has ended and whether the module's last piece of evidence was still in it — so that the engine does not work through an error or give probes within the series, nor work through the error of the series' last answer either. The model counts the series, and the engine does not repeat its rules. The output type of `Read` since T15 carries no such signs, and the state does not remember whether the last piece of evidence was still in the series. This is T20, and how the state learns it is decided in its planning;
  - concepts 15 spec — "virtual learner" and "population": `sim` has populations, not profiles, because a profile is a learner in an account. The card architecture 3.9 spec calls the detailing of `sim` finished (R03–R05 sim);
  - the rest between the tags — `COORDINATION.md`, the 2026-11-06 and 2026-11-07 amendments to R93 spec about the sample file and the recipe `sample-check`, the examples of eval contracts — does not concern `learner-model`. The template, `.devcontainer/`, RULES.md and the documents check did not change.
- **What is changed here:**
  - `spec/` — to v0.9.5, commit 86f442a; the tag in CLAUDE.md, README.md and at the start of RUN.md;
  - the template and `.devcontainer/` — unchanged: `git -C spec diff v0.9.3 v0.9.5` does not touch `repo-template/` and `.devcontainer/`, so a three-way merge gives nothing, and the image is the same;
  - CLAUDE.md and README.md — [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 2, no longer has an open question, and the first version comes out before the first tag of `sim`. README.md — also the state of the code: T16 did not update it, and it said there was no code of the model, though since T16 there are the level and "mastered". The edit is in the same paragraph as the answer to О-25;
  - [architecture.md](architecture.md):
    - section 1 — the gate by R103 spec and the populations, and the first version comes out before the first tag of `sim`;
    - section 2 — subsection 2.7 is decided;
    - 3.1 and 3.7 — the signs of the trial series in `Read`;

    - 3.3 — the way of input does not change the weight;
  - RUN.md:

    - the rows В23 and В24 are gone from table 3, and its opening says what closed them.

## The first release

**R16. The release of `learner-model` is made by the shared `release.yml` of `.github` on the line `0.1`, starting with `v0.1.0`, when a commit of `main` with green CI changes the code, the schema or what the file is built with. Go consumers take the code as a Go module by tag, and the release's only file is the schema archive `learner-model-proto-<version>.tar.gz`, signed keylessly; `spec`'s `release-verify` checks it. Immutable releases and a ruleset of the tags `v*` are turned on before the first release, and the control of the rules test is no longer a nested module, so that the Go module zip carries the whole test suite.** The author's answers of 2026-11-07 in planning T21 (RUN.md), all three by the plan's recommendations.
- **What is decided:**
  - **the release file** is one: the schema archive `learner-model-proto-<version>.tar.gz`. In it is a folder of the same name with `LICENSE`, `buf.yaml` and all the `.proto` from `proto/`, in a tar that every build writes the same way: names in order, without times and owners, mode 0644, gzip without a name and a time. This is the description of the form of evidence and state for those who read them without Go — for the export of a learner's data (the note on epic E4 in table 2 of `spec`'s RUN.md; [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.5, "Export");
  - **the code** — as the Go module `github.com/altnovatrail/learner-model` by the release's tag, through the proxy and the checksum database, as the module of `spec` (R64 spec): `go get github.com/altnovatrail/learner-model@v0.1.0`. It is not a release file, and `release-verify` does not touch it, while `go.sum` and sum.golang.org hold it;
  - **the control of the rules test** keeps its `go.mod` under the name `go.mod.control`, and the rules test reads it under that name. The control's `go.sum` is deleted: it was needed only by SonarCloud, whose rule S8566 looks at a `go.mod` with `require`. `TestNoNestedModules` holds that there is one nested module below the root — the lock file of gopls `.devcontainer/gopls` (R14), which neither the code nor a test reads —, while the zip carries neither the submodule `spec` nor the folders of version control systems anyway;
  - **the settings** were turned on by the executor through `gh api`, before the merge of T21's pull request: immutable releases — `enabled: true` — and the ruleset `v-tags`, id 26770474: `refs/tags/v*`, `update`, `deletion`, `non_fast_forward`, without bypass, a copy of `module-kit`'s 25559103. Checked against the API's answers 2026-11-07.
- **A proposal of the plan and of the execution, not the author's answer:**
  - **`release.yml`** — as in `module-kit`: `workflow_run` of CI on `main` and a button, permissions `contents: write`, `id-token: write`, `actions: read`, the shared workflow v0.1.15 by SHA. `RELEASE_LINE` is `0.1`: the line's first release is `v0.1.0`, as R12 named it, and before v1.0.0 the API may change;
  - **`paths`** — the code without tests and `testdata/`, `go.mod`, `proto/`, `buf.yaml`, `LICENSE`, `justfile`, `.devcontainer/Dockerfile` and `release.yml` itself. Tests, golden files and documents give no release: the module does not change by them for whoever imports it;
  - **the recipes** — `release-files <version>`, which the shared release calls, and `ci-release` in the job "Proto": two builds — the same bytes, the archive's content — `LICENSE`, `buf.yaml` and all the `.proto` git knows, as in the tree, and `buf build` from the unpacked archive. `ci-release` does not run beside a ready `dist/`: `release-files` would replace it;
  - **`buf breaking`** from the first tag checks the schema against it — `ci-proto` takes the latest tag `v*`, as the 2026-11-07 amendment to R10 decided.

— *Why:*
- **A Go module, not a file.** `sim`, `engine` and `api` are code in Go, and `go.mod` with `go.sum` pin the version themselves, while the checksum database keeps the hash of the zip forever. The module of `spec` is already taken so (R64 spec), and a second route would only split the version in two.
- **The file is the schema.** The shared release needs at least one file in `dist/`, and the schema is the only thing needed without Go: for the export, so that the log can be read without the library (R09 spec; [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.5). The signature and the attestation of an immutable release link it with the tag's commit (R77 spec).
- **A reproducible tar.** The bytes do not depend on the build's time and owner, so the file can be built again from the tag and compared, and `ci-release` holds this from every pull request.
- **A zip without nested modules.** "Module zip files do not include the contents of vendor directories or any nested modules (subdirectories that contain go.mod files)" (Go Modules Reference, Module zip files; the quote of R64 spec). The control with `go.mod` would not get into the zip, and `TestCodeRulesControl` would fail in the consumer's module cache, while the zip of a version can no longer be fixed.
- **The settings before the first release** are the condition of the check of R77 spec: "a repository turns them on before its first release".

— *Rejected:*
- an archive of the module's whole source — it repeats the Go module, which consumers take by tag anyway; both archives;
- leaving the control a nested module and recording a debt — v0.1.0 would forever carry a failing test;
- turning on the settings by the author's hands — the executor turns them on and checks them against the API's answer, as the ruleset `main` in R13;
- a trial run of the shared release with `publish: false` on a pull request — `ci-release` holds the build of the file, and the shared release checks the signature itself after publishing;
- tests and `testdata/` in `paths` — the module zip carries them too, but a release on every edit of a test or a golden file would give consumers versions in which nothing changed for import. An edit of a test comes out with the next release of code, as in `module-kit`, whose `paths` are also without `*_test.go`.

— *Consequence:* RUN.md, T21 — the first release comes out by the merge of its pull request, and it is irreversible: the merge comes with a separate confirmation of the author; the outcome, the checks and the row «в реестр spec» about the released version — by a second pull request. [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.6 and 3.7; README — the section on releases. — `.github/workflows/release.yml`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `justfile`, `rules_test.go`, `testdata/coderules/`.

— *Amendment 2026-11-07:* so it came out. **R18. The checks of the same bits go by the shared jobs of `go.yml` of `.github` v0.1.21 with the input `same-bits` — "Go / targets", "Go / fma-diff", "Go / arm64" and "Go / rules" —, and the lists of the code rules are `.github/coderules.json`. The own recipes `test-targets` and `fma-diff`, the job "Go arm64", `rules_test.go` with the control `testdata/coderules` and the control `testdata/fma` are removed. All the shared workflows are on v0.1.21. Debt Д13 is closed.** The author's decision of 2026-11-08 — close Д13; a task outside an epic, T26 of RUN.md.
- **What this is.** R107 spec made the checks that R09 held as its own shared by `learner-model` and `engine`: the runs on the targets and `fma-diff` from `.github` v0.1.18, the job arm64 from v0.1.19, the code rules test — as the tool `tools/coderules/` with the caller's lists — from v0.1.20 (R16 and R33 .github). The tool is a superset of the copies: beyond the rules of `rules_test.go` it holds `replace` in `go.mod` and nested modules by the list, and its control for `learner-model` is `tools/coderules/testdata/learner-model`, the same module as the former `testdata/coderules`, except the comments (`diff -r`, 2026-11-08).
- **The lists** (`.github/coderules.json`): the code's imports — the former list of [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.6, without `math/bits`, which no file imports; the tests' imports — `bytes`, `crypto/sha256`, `fmt`, `math/rand/v2`, `os`, `path/filepath`, `runtime` and `testing`, without `go/token`, `go/parser`, `go/ast`, `go/types` and `go/importer`, which only the rules test needed. `runtime` is needed by the tests of `detmath`: in wasm and on 386 the port is compared with `math` bit for bit; the tests' functions of `math` are `Exp` and `Log`; `requires` is empty; the nested module is `.devcontainer/gopls` (R14).
- **`ci-test`** is only the run with `-race` and coverage into `coverage/go.out`. "Go / arm64" calls it on `ubuntu-24.04-arm` and then its host's runs, so the former job "Go arm64" would repeat it.
- **The ruleset `main`** requires, instead of "Go arm64", four checks of `same-bits` — "Go / targets", "Go / fma-diff", "Go / arm64" and "Go / rules" — with `integration_id` 15368, after their green run on pull request #33 of T26; the other rules do not change. Without this the merge would wait for "Go arm64", which no longer exists. The author puts them: the classifier of Claude Code's auto mode forbade an edit of the ruleset from the executor's session as a bypass of CI, 2026-11-08.
- **Checked** (2026-11-08, in the container on amd64): `just ci-lint` and `just ci-test` green, `go mod tidy -diff` empty; the scenarios "Go / targets" and "Go / fma-diff" from `go.yml` v0.1.21 — green, `fma-diff` ok on six targets; the tool v0.1.21, built from a clone of `.github` in a temporary folder, on the module with the lists — 0 findings, and on `a*b + c` in the code and an import of `go/ast` in a test — two findings, `fused` and `import`, and exit code 1.

— *Why:* the two libraries have the same checks, and two copies of the rules test have already diverged — `engine` has the rule `replace`, which was not here (R33 .github) —, and would diverge further. The lists are the repository's data, while the rules are one (R107 spec). The shared workflows are on one version, as R13 requires, and v0.1.21 is the latest release of `.github` (`gh release list -R altnovatrail/.github`, 2026-11-08).

— *Rejected:*
- v0.1.20: v0.1.21 has the same function, with the record of T48 and the archive of E14, and the move takes the latest release;
- keeping an own `rules_test.go` beside "Go / rules": two copies of the same rules, against which R107 spec decided;
- keeping the recipes `test-targets` and `fma-diff` for running in the container by hand: a third copy of the scenarios of `go.yml`, which would diverge from it;
- the tests' import lists as in the control of `.github`, with the packages `go/*`: the tests no longer need them, and a list wider than needed lets extra through;
- bypassing the release `v0.1.1` by not touching the `justfile` and `release.yml`: `ci-test` would call the removed recipes, and the shared workflows would stand at different versions.

— *Consequence:* `.github/coderules.json`, `.github/workflows/*.yml`, `justfile`; `rules_test.go`, `testdata/coderules/` and `testdata/fma/` are deleted; the 2026-11-08 amendments to R09, R13 and R16; CLAUDE.md, README.md; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3, 3.6 and 4; RUN.md, Д10 and Д11; RUN.md, T26, Д13 closed. The merge gives `v0.1.1` (the 2026-11-08 amendment to R16).

## The gate of `sim`

**R20. The gate of `sim` is the job "Gate" in `ci.yml`: `sim` from git by the tag and the SHA of its commit, written in the job's `env`, with the tag checked by `git ls-remote`, and `sim`'s recipe `just gate` in the stage `toolchain` of `sim`'s devcontainer of the same tag, with the pull request's code in `GATE_LEARNER_MODEL`. It runs on every pull request and every commit of `main`, on `ubuntu-24.04`, with `timeout-minutes: 15`, without a Go cache, and "Gate" is a required check of the ruleset of `main`. The first tag is `sim` `v0.1.0`.** The author's decision of 2026-11-09 in planning T22 is to merge T26 and T27 first, and to check the red gate both in the container and in CI; the rest is the plan's proposal, which the author approved (RUN.md, T22).
- **What stands** (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`, job `gate`):
  - `env` — `SIM_TAG: v0.1.0` and `SIM_COMMIT: 09e5a84f8a26fedcf5c8996bf81bf29c616917a2`. The checkout of this repository is in `lm/`, `altnovatrail/sim` by `SIM_COMMIT` in `sim/`, both without credentials;
  - the step "sim tag" — the tag from `git ls-remote` must name `SIM_COMMIT`, an annotated one through `^{}`, or the job fails and names both. This is how `sim`'s recipe checks the tags of the libraries against `gate.json`;
  - the image — `docker build --target toolchain - < sim/.devcontainer/Dockerfile`;
  - the step "gate" — `docker run` on the same pattern as the job "Proto", with `--env GATE_LEARNER_MODEL=/work/lm` and `--workdir /work/sim`: `just gate`, the mode `bands`, and the table of the numbers against the bands goes to `$GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY`, with `pipefail`. The recipe writes `go.work` in a temporary folder — `use` of `sim`, of the pull request's code and of `engine` at the commit from `gate.json` —, does not commit it and does not touch the harness's `go.mod`. Code 0 means the gate is green, 1 that it is red or the input is rejected, 2 an unknown call;
  - the ruleset of `main` requires "Gate" with `integration_id` 15368, after its green run on the pull request of T22. The executor made the edit of the ruleset with the author's permission on 2026-11-09 — and the replacement of "Go arm64" by four checks `same-bits` by R18 too.
- **Checked** (2026-11-09):
  - in the container, on a clone of `sim` at `09e5a84` in the session's folder: `GATE_LEARNER_MODEL=/workspaces/learner-model just gate` — code 0 in 53 seconds, "The gate is green: every number is within their bands, and no learner breaks an invariant". With `bandStep = 3` instead of 2.5 in `model.go` — code 1, "The gate is red", and outside the bands, among others, `M1.rms.200` — 0.603 against the band 0.380 ± 2 × 0.020 and `M3.lag` — −0.726 against −1.242 ± 2 × 0.038. The edit was rolled back;
  - the step "sim tag" with the same text: with `v0.1.0` and `09e5a84` — 0, with another SHA and with a tag that does not exist — 1, and both are named;
  - CI is in RUN.md, T22, «Сделано».

— *Why:*
- **The tag and the SHA in `env`, not a reusable workflow of `sim`.** So R103 spec and `sim`'s README, "Taking a release", require, and `sim` deliberately gives no reusable workflow: the gate would half live in `sim` (R05 `sim`, "Rejected"). The SHA holds the code, the tag the reader and the check: a tag moved by mistake will not pass silently.
- **`sim`'s recipe, not a `go.work` of its own.** `just gate` already writes `go.work`, takes `engine` from `gate.json`, checks the build and the bands, and `GATE_LEARNER_MODEL` is its input for the caller (R26 `sim`). A recipe of its own would be a copy that would drift from it.
- **`sim`'s stage `toolchain`, not one of its own.** The gate checks the line `go` and the Go it is built with — R103 spec names `sim`'s devcontainer of the same tag, and then Go, just and git are the same as the harness's.
- **Only amd64, without a cache.** The same bits on amd64 and arm64 are held by `sim`'s CI with its `Gate / arm64`, and `ubuntu-24.04` is enough for the caller (3.9 `sim`). A Go cache, by the measurement of T24 `sim`, gives almost nothing, and the cache key is one more thing to keep.
- **On every pull request.** So R103 spec requires: an edit of a document will not move the gate, and to keep a list of the paths that call it would mean guessing what the model reads. Four minutes of a public repository's runner cost nothing (R30 spec).

— *Rejected:*
- a reusable `gate.yml` in `sim` or in `.github` — `sim` rejected it (R05 `sim`), and R103 spec names the job the caller's own;
- `go mod edit -replace` instead of `go.work` — it edits the harness's `go.mod`, rejected by R103 spec;
- a matrix of amd64 and arm64, as in `sim`'s CI — a repeat of what `sim` already holds;
- a Go cache on `sim`'s pattern;
- the gate only on commits that change the code — paths outside the release's `paths` pass the gate anyway, and the rule "on every pull request" is R103 spec.

— *Consequence:* `.github/workflows/ci.yml`; the ruleset of `main`; the 2026-11-09 amendments to R12 and R15; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 1, 3.6 and 4; README.md, CLAUDE.md; RUN.md, T22 and row В30. A candidate that moves the numbers goes by the order of releases of R103 spec: first the bands in `sim` and its new tag, then the raise of `SIM_TAG` and `SIM_COMMIT` here. `ci.yml` is not in the release's `paths`, so the merge of T22 gives no release.

## The leading question of format 0.4

**R21. The leading question of a task of format 0.4 is help, as a technique on request: the answer after it goes into the step, the counters and the stability as it is, but does not set "mastered". The evidence carries it by a field of its own, apart from `technique_on_request`, and this is a fact of the attempt. The code comes with format 0.4.** The author's answer of 2026-11-09 to В26 (RUN.md, T27), by option 1 — the recommendation of [architecture.md](architecture.md), subsection 2.8.
- **What is decided:** the evidence has an optional field "the learner got a leading question", beside the technique on request; `api` gives the question for a task, not for an item, before the first wrong answer (the 2026-11-08 amendment to R82 spec). If it is present, every answer of the attempt is with help: the same rule as for a technique on request (R06; [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.3). There is no new number. The field's name and its number in the schema are given by the code's task;
- **when:** with the move to a tag of `spec` of the line 0.10, where format 0.4 will come out, together with the value `time` of `CheckKind` (R19) — a change after the baseline, through the gate of `sim` (R12, R20): debt Д14 in table 4 of RUN.md. The field comes out in a release of `learner-model` before `api` starts writing it, and `api` writes the question only with it, not with `technique_on_request` (row В27).

— *Why:* the question hints at the method more strongly than a technique — that is why the format closed it (R57 spec; R108 spec, В31) —, so it weighs no less than a technique, and help is the strongest that R06 gives without a new number. A field of its own keeps the fact apart: the log is append-only, and if the harness shows that the question must weigh otherwise than a technique, a new version will read it by recomputation (R08).

— *Rejected:*
- the same rule by the field `technique_on_request` — the log would mix the two facts forever, and splitting them by recomputation would no longer be possible;
- nothing, as a technique at the step — the answer after a question that hinted at the method would set "mastered";
- a smaller weight of the answer — one more constant without data, which R06 rejected.

— *Consequence:* [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 2, 2.8 and 3.3; README.md, CLAUDE.md; RUN.md — row В26 left table 3, Д14 and row В27 name the decision. The code and the schema come with the task of the move to a tag of the line 0.10.

— *Amendment 2026-11-09:* **"when" and "The code and the schema come with the task of the move to a tag of the line 0.10" above read so: by a task of its own after the move, through the gate of `sim`, in one pull request and one release.** The move came as task T34 to `spec` v0.12.0, without code (R23): format 0.4 is already there, and the task has nothing more to wait for. The weight, the field and the order — the release before `api` starts writing the field — are the same, and `spec` recorded them by the 2026-11-09 amendment to R82 spec, by row В27. — R23; RUN.md, T34 and Д14.

— *Amendment 2026-11-11:* **the field is `leading_question_on_request = 20` of the evidence of kind 2, in Go `Evidence.LeadingQuestionOnRequest`; it came with task T37 (R28).** The weight is the same: help, as a technique on request, through the rule `helped`. — R28; RUN.md, T37.

## The plan of E3

**R22. Epic E3 is the first candidates of the model through the gate of `sim`. Up to three candidates on the numbers Д5–Д7 go into `sim`'s first judgement, each by its own draft pull request, and an exploration chooses them — rough runs of the harness in the container. The numbers that `engine` shares — the threshold "mastered" and the boundary of failure ½ — do not go into the first judgement. The test vectors of standalone that pin an old number are removed by the candidate, with a record.** The author's answers of 2026-11-09 in planning E3, all four by the plan's recommendations (RUN.md, table 1).
- **What was found in planning** (`sim` on `main` a1bf796, read 2026-11-09):
  - `sim`'s E3 is split into T29–T35. The judgement of a candidate of the model is T32 `sim`, written for one candidate, though the file of the judgement names the candidates as a list (R25 `sim`) — how many it judges at a time is asked by row В31. It waits for a new pin and tag of `sim` — T30, `modules` v0.1.4 and new paths —, for the deciding run on the candidate's commit through `go.work` — T31 — and for the candidate's pull request here. `sim` will not judge candidates on `v0.1.0`, and the gates of the libraries move to the tag of T30 (В9 RUN.md `sim`);
  - the judgement is a deciding run, 4,000 learners per cell, about 35 minutes in the container on 20 cores, then `sim judge` by the model's criterion: the constraints "no worse" and the estimate of the groups. A candidate names its group and its target, the number of changed constants and a new field, if there is one (R25 `sim`). Of those that passed the simplest is chosen, and the chosen one is confirmed on held-out learners — otherwise the exit is to the base (R04 `sim`);
  - the numbers Д5–Д7 are named constants of `model.go`, but for ρ, the boundary ½, the halving of τ and the guessing of `predicate`, which stand in formulas, and C, hard-wired in `detmath` and in `reviewDate`. There is no constant of the model's version in the code: the cache's mark is the release's tag (R80 spec).
- **What is decided:**
  1. up to three candidates go into the first judgement, each by its own draft pull request: one constant or a coherent set, with its own group of the estimate. If `sim` judges one at a time (В31), they go in turn, the best by the exploration first;
  2. the candidates are chosen by an exploration — rough runs of `sim` on the numbers Д5–Д7, one at a time, on fewer learners than a deciding run. They decide nothing but show which number moves which measure; the result is an entry of the log with the candidates and the rejected ones. The constants step both ways, and the edges — guessing 0 and full weight — only inwards. The rule of τ by the share of correct answers and C do not go into the exploration: the first is a rule, not a number, and C is hard-wired into a formula and into an exported constant of `detmath`, and changing it is the model's design and the API of `detmath` (the plan, the executor's proposal after review);
  3. the threshold "mastered" only in a pair with the target of `engine`'s corridor, and the boundary ½ is read by `engine` too, with R107 spec: neither is in the first judgement, Д16 and Д7;
  4. the vectors of standalone that pin an old number are removed by the candidate, with a record in the log. The baseline with them is held by the tag `v0.1.0`, and the new one by the gate and the golden vectors.
- **The tasks** are T29–T33 of RUN.md: the gate on the tag of T30 `sim`, the exploration, the candidates, the judgement and the release, the result.

— *Why:* of those that passed, `sim`'s criterion chooses the simplest, so several candidates in one judgement give more chances than one, and a deciding run for each takes about 35 minutes. An exploration is cheaper than a judgement, and without it the judgement would more likely reject all. A pair with `engine` needs its candidate and a paired criterion, and R103 spec does not name how to release the boundary ½ together with `engine` (R17); the first judgement does not wait for it. The vectors of standalone are printed for standalone's numbers: a candidate that changes a number is not supposed to pass them, and keeping them on parameters would mean testing code that does not go into the release.

— *Rejected:*
- one candidate — if it does not pass, one more round of judgement is needed;
- candidates by reasoning, without runs — faster, but blind;
- the pair of the threshold and the target of the corridor in the first judgement — it needs synchronous work with `engine`'s session and its candidate;
- the model's constants as an internal set, so that the vectors of standalone run on its numbers — more code, and a separate task would have to go first.

— *Consequence:* RUN.md — E3 in table 1 with T29–T33, the row of E3 left table 2, Д16, «Когда вернуться» of Д5–Д7 and row В31; CLAUDE.md, README.md and [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 1 — E3 is under way.

— *Amendment 2026-11-10:* **the "about 35 minutes" of a deciding run above is the time of the old pin: on `olympiad-math` 0.1.1 the run of the base takes 6 h 43 min on 20 cores, and that of the engine's ceiling 1 h 31 min (RUN.md `sim`, T30). This does not change decisions 1–4: there is one judgement per round of candidates (the 2026-11-09 amendment to R103 spec), and an exploration is all the cheaper than it.** Fitting the run into an hour is T36 `sim`, before the deciding run of the candidates (T31 `sim`). — R24; RUN.md, table 1.

**R23. `learner-model` stands on `spec` v0.12.0, the latest release. The tag answers all four rows «в реестр spec» of this repository — В27, В28, В30 and В31 —, all by their recommendations, and the rows are closed. It also brings the released format 0.4, but the move does not change the model's code: the field of the leading question and the value `time` of `CheckKind` go by a task of their own, through the gate of `sim` (R12) — debt Д14. `.devcontainer/` is the output of the recipe `new-repo`, and it differs from the former one only in the comment of the gopls step.** The author's decision of 2026-11-09 is to move to the latest tag and to take the changes through to the merge into `main` himself; a task outside an epic, T34 of RUN.md.
- **What the tag brings `learner-model`** (`git -C spec diff v0.9.16 v0.12.0`, read 2026-11-09; Д135–Д140 in table 4 of RUN.md `spec`; `COORDINATION.md`):
  - the answer to В27 (Д138): the 2026-11-09 amendment to R82 spec — "as it carries a technique" speaks of the fact being recorded, not of the field: the evidence carries the leading question by a field of its own in `learner-model`'s schema, apart from the technique on request (R21), and `api` writes it only with this field and only after the release of `learner-model` that brings the field in. The same is written in concepts 10 spec and in the card architecture 3.8 spec — on the weights of evidence, together with `time` without guessing (R19), and on the evidence itself. The contract `novatrail.api.v1` got the fields `GetHintResponse.leading_question` and `OpenAttempt.requested_leading_question`, but that is a conversation between `app` and `api`, not evidence;
  - the answer to В28 (Д139): the comment of the gopls step in `repo-template/kinds/go/Dockerfile` now says that after its creation a copy is held only by the repository's own check, if it has one. Here that is the check in `ci-lint` (R19);
  - the answer to В30 (Д136): row 4.8 of `COORDINATION.md` is closed — "Gate" stands at `learner-model` and `engine` —, and the first candidates are a new row 4.9: a new pin of `sim` — `modules` `v0.1.4`, `engine` `v0.1.3` and `learner-model` `v0.1.3` (Д137), a tag of `sim` with it (T30 `sim`), then the gate here on that tag — T29;
  - the answer to В31 (Д140): the 2026-11-09 amendment to R103 spec — `sim` judges the candidates of one library in a round: a deciding run on the last commit of each one's pull request, one judgement with all of them, of those that passed the simplest, a confirmation only of the chosen one. This is the order E3 is designed for (R22);
  - format 0.4 came out with v0.10.0, the first release of the line 0.10 (the 2026-11-09 amendment to R44 spec; [the format's text](../spec/format/README.md), section 2), with the content by R108 and R111 spec. Д135 names as `learner-model`'s its parts Д121 and Д138 — the same that Д14 holds;
  - the rest between the tags — R112–R123 spec on the legal questions of the analysis, the learner's artifacts, development agents, self-hosting, the grounds of processing and consent, DPIA, eval keys and AI providers, the part `deployment/` of `spec` and the workbook page in the format, the edits of privacy, product, repositories and of `spec`'s release recipes — does not concern `learner-model`: the library stores nothing and sees no learner's data but what `api` passes to it (privacy 4 spec). The template `repo-template/files/`, RULES.md, `spec`'s `.devcontainer/`, the documents check and the recipe `new-repo` did not change.
- **What is changed here:**
  - `spec/` — at v0.12.0, commit 5b4640a; `gh release verify v0.12.0 -R altnovatrail/spec` — "Release v0.12.0 verified!", the release is immutable and the latest (`gh release view` and `gh release list`, 2026-11-09). The tag is in CLAUDE.md, README.md and at the top of RUN.md;

It differs from the former one only in the comment of the gopls step in `Dockerfile`; the `ARG`, `COPY` and `RUN` are the same, and so is the image;
